Low regularity well-posedness for two-dimensional hydroelastic waves
Lizhe Wan, Jiaqi Yang

Abstract
. We investigate the low regularity local well-posedness of two-dimensional irrotational deep hydroelastic waves. Building on the approach of Ifrim-Tataru [ 29 ] and Ai-Ifrim-Tataru [ 3 ] , in particular by constructing a cubic modified energy that incorporates a paradifferential weight chosen carefully, we prove that the hydroelastic waves are locally well-posed in ℋ s {\mathcal{H}}^{s} for s > 3 4 s>\frac{3}{4} .



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
76B15, 74F10, 76B07

1.
Introduction

The
hydroelastic wave problem
describes the interaction between elastic structures and hydrodynamic excitation.
It arises in a wide range of applications, including biology, medical science, and ocean engineering; see, for example,
[
30
,
46
]
and the references therein.
Based on the Cosserat shell theory under Kirchhoff’s hypotheses, which accounts for both bending stresses in the sheet and membrane-stretching tension, Toland
[
47
]
introduced a fully nonlinear elastic model for two-dimensional hydroelastic waves with a clear Hamiltonian structure. This model was later extended to three spatial dimensions by Plotnikov & Toland
[
42
]
.
A comprehensive review by Părău
et al.

[
32
,
41
]
summarizes recent advances in the analysis, numerical simulation, experimentation, and applications of hydroelastic waves.

Figure 1.
Hydroelastic waves

In this paper, we investigate a hydroelastic model in which a two-dimensional, inviscid, incompressible fluid undergoes irrotational motion beneath a frictionless thin elastic sheet, as illustrated in Figure
1
. A typical example of this configuration occurs in polar regions, where water freezes to form an ice sheet during winter. Such ice sheets are often used as roads or runways and can subsequently be fractured by air-cushioned vehicles.

We begin by recalling its mathematical formulation. The fluid domain at time
t
is denoted by
\Omega_{t}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
, defined as the region below the graph of a function
\eta:\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\Omega_{t}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y<\eta(t,x)\}.

Its free boundary, corresponding to the deformed elastic sheet, is given by

\Sigma_{t}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:y=\eta(t,x)\}.

In the absence of gravity, the fluid motion is governed by the following system:

(1.1)

\begin{cases}\mathbf{u}_{t}+\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}=-\nabla p&\text{in }\Omega_{t},\\
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}=0,\quad\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{u}=0&\text{in }\Omega_{t},\\
\eta_{t}=u_{2}-u_{1}\eta_{x}&\text{on }\Sigma_{t},\\
p=\sigma\mathbf{E}(\eta)&\text{on }\Sigma_{t},\\
\mathbf{u}(0,x)=\mathbf{u}_{0}(x)&\text{in }\Omega_{t},\end{cases}

where
\mathbf{u}=(u_{1},u_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
denotes the fluid velocity,
p
is the pressure, and
\sigma
represents the coefficient of flexural rigidity. The term

(1.2)

\sigma\mathbf{E}(\eta)=\sigma\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\eta_{x}^{2}}}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\eta_{x}^{2}}}\left(\frac{\eta_{xx}}{(1+\eta_{x}^{2})^{3/2}}\right)_{x}\right]_{x}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\eta_{xx}}{(1+\eta_{x}^{2})^{3/2}}\right)^{3}\right\}

represents the restoring force generated by the elastic sheet, expressed as a pressure jump across the interface;
see Toland
et al.

[
47
,
42
]
, Guyenne and Părău
[
24
]
and Groves
et al.

[
1
,
23
]
for related discussions.

Given the irrotational condition
\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{u}=0
, we may introduce a velocity potential
\phi
such that
\mathbf{u}=\nabla\phi
.
Since
\phi
solves the Laplace equation, by the theory of elliptic PDEs, it suffices to consider its evolution on the boundary
\Gamma_{t}
.
Let
\varphi(t,x)=\phi(t,x,\eta(t,x))
denote the trace of the velocity potential on the free surface, the kinematic and dynamic boundary conditions ((
1.1
)
3
and (
1.1
)
4
) become:

(1.3)

\displaystyle\eta_{t}

\displaystyle=(\phi_{y}-\eta_{x}\phi_{x})|_{y=\eta(t,x)}=:G(\eta)\varphi,

(1.4)

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\phi_{y}^{2}-\phi_{x}^{2}\right)-\eta_{x}\phi_{x}\phi_{y}-p\right]_{y=\eta(t,x)}=-\frac{1}{2}\varphi_{x}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\eta_{x}\varphi_{x}+G(\eta)\varphi)^{2}}{1+\eta^{2}_{x}}-\sigma\mathbf{E}(\eta).

The system of (
1.3
) and (
1.4
) is the well-known Zakharov-Craig-Sulem formulation; see
[
58
,
21
]
.
The aim of this paper is to establish the
low-regularity well-posedness
of the hydroelastic waves.
We begin by recalling some known well-posedness results for water waves and hydroelastic waves.

1.1.
Local well-posedness results of water waves and hydroelastic waves.

The local well-posedness of water waves has been extensively studied.
These studies typically consider models in which the elastic term
\sigma\mathbf{E}(\eta)
in (
1.4
) is replaced by one of the following:

•

a gravity term
g\eta
(where
g
is the gravitational acceleration),

•

a capillary term
-\kappa\eta_{xx}/(1+\eta_{x}^{2})^{3/2}
(where
\kappa
is the coefficient of surface tension),

•

or a combination of both.

The small-data problem was first treated by Nalimov
[
39
]
& Yoshihara
[
56
,
57
]
. A major breakthrough for the local well-posedness with general data was achieved by Wu
[
53
,
54
]
.
Subsequent developments include the works of Christodoulou & Lindblad
[
18
]
, Lannes
[
33
]
, Coutand & Shkoller
[
20
]
, Ambrose & Masmoudi
[
13
,
14
]
, Shatah & Zeng
[
44
,
43
,
45
]
, Alazard, Burq & Zuily
[
7
,
8
]
, Hunter, Ifrim & Tataru
[
26
]
, Ifrim & Tataru
[
29
]
, Ai
[
4
,
5
]
, and Ai, Ifrim & Tataru
[
3
]
.

Regarding the Strichartz estimates of water waves, Christianson
et al.

[
17
]
, Alazard
et al.

[
9
,
10
]
, de Poyferré & Nguyen
[
22
]
, Nguyen
[
40
]
, and Ai
[
4
,
5
,
6
]
proved the Strichartz estimate arising directly from the dispersive property of free-surface water waves.

In contrast to water waves, the well-posedness theory for hydroelastic waves remains less developed. Initial progress was made by Ambrose & Siegel
[
15
]
, who established the local well-posedness of two-dimensional hydroelastic waves using a vortex sheet formulation. This was extended by Liu & Ambrose
[
34
]
, who incorporated the mass of the elastic sheet into the analysis. In later work
[
35
]
, the same authors further examined the asymptotic behavior of two-dimensional hydroelastic waves with respect to various physical parameters. Recently, the second author and Wang
[
52
]
proved the local well-posedness of hydroelastic waves with vorticity in arbitrary spatial dimensions. Nevertheless, to the best of our knowledge, there are no results addressing the low regularity problem for hydroelastic waves.

1.2.
Hydroelastic waves in holomorphic coordinates

In
[
26
,
29
]
, holomorphic coordinates were used to develop the well-posedness theory for both deep gravity and capillary water waves, respectively.
This formulation was also used to study a variety of other water wave problems; see
[
26
,
28
,
25
,
3
,
2
,
27
,
49
,
50
]
.
In Section
2
of Yang
[
55
]
, the second author derived the two-dimensional deep hydroelastic wave equations in holomorphic coordinates.
We refer the interested reader to
[
55
]
for the detailed derivation.
Although other formulations may be applicable, we utilize holomorphic coordinates to study the well-posedness theory in this paper.

Let
\mathbf{P}:=\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{I}-iH)
be the holomorphic projection that selects the holomorphic portion of the complex-valued function, with
H
being the Hilbert transform.
On the Fourier side,
{\mathbf{P}}
projects onto the negative frequencies.
The complex conjugate operator
\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}
then projects onto the anti-holomorphic component.

Let
W
denote the holomorphic position variable and
Q
the holomorphic velocity potential.
These functions are defined on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\alpha}
and take values in
\mathbb{C}
.
Then, according to the derivations in
[
55
]
, the free boundary irrotational Euler equations (
1.1
) and (
1.2
) are equivalent to the following system of equations:

(1.5)

\begin{cases}&W_{t}+F(1+W_{\alpha})=0,\\
&Q_{t}+FQ_{\alpha}+{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{|Q_{\alpha}|^{2}}{J}\right]-i\sigma{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{W_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+W_{\alpha})}-\frac{\bar{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{W}_{\alpha})}\right)\right]\right\}\\
&-\frac{i}{2}\sigma{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\left[\frac{W_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+W_{\alpha})}-\frac{\bar{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{W}_{\alpha})}\right]^{3}\right\}=0,\end{cases}

where
J:=|1+W_{\alpha}|^{2}
is the Jacobian, and
F={\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{Q_{\alpha}-\bar{Q}_{\alpha}}{J}\right]
.
The system (
1.5
) is fully nonlinear.
By taking the
\alpha
derivative and diagonalizing, we introduce the differentiated variables

(1.6)

\mathbf{W}=W_{\alpha},\quad\,R=\frac{Q_{\alpha}}{1+W_{\alpha}}.

The pair
(\mathbf{W},R)
solves the differentiated system

(1.7)

\begin{cases}&\mathbf{W}_{t}+b\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\frac{(1+\mathbf{W})R_{\alpha}}{(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}=(1+\mathbf{W})M,\\
&R_{t}+bR_{\alpha}+\frac{ia}{1+\mathbf{W}}\\
&-\frac{i\sigma}{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}\right)\right]\right\}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{i\sigma}{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{\mathbf{W}^{3}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{3}}-\frac{3\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}{J^{\frac{5}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}\right]_{\alpha}\\
&=-\frac{i\sigma}{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right)\right]\right\}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{i\sigma}{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{3}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{3}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})^{3}}-\frac{3\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}{J^{\frac{5}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right]_{\alpha},\end{cases}

where the real-valued
frequency-shift

a
and the
advection velocity

b
are given by

(1.8)

a:=i\left(\bar{P}[\bar{R}R_{\alpha}]-P[R\bar{R}_{\alpha}]\right),\,\quad b:={\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{R}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\right]+\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}\left[\frac{\bar{R}}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right].

The auxiliary function
M
is given by

(1.9)

M:=\frac{R_{\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}+\frac{\bar{R}_{\alpha}}{1+\mathbf{W}}-b_{\alpha}=\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}[\bar{R}Y_{\alpha}-R_{\alpha}\bar{Y}]+{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}-\bar{R}_{\alpha}Y].

Here we use the notation
Y:=\frac{\mathbf{W}}{1+\mathbf{W}}
.
The variable
R
also has an intrinsic meaning: it represents the complex conjugate of the complex velocity evaluated at (or restricted to) the water surface.

We remark that (
1.7
) is a diagonal and self-contained system.
It is invariant under spatial translations and admits the scaling

(1.10)

\left(\mathbf{W}(t,\alpha),R(t,\alpha)\right)\to\left(\mathbf{W}(\lambda^{\frac{5}{2}}t,\lambda\alpha),\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}}R(\lambda^{\frac{5}{2}}t,\lambda\alpha)\right).

In the remainder of this paper, we study the low-regularity well-posedness of (
1.7
).
For simplicity, the flexural rigidity coefficient
\sigma
is normalized to be
1
.
We also note that the setting and analysis in the periodic case differ slightly from those on the real line; see Appendix
A.4
in
[
26
]
for an explanation.
We focus exclusively on the analysis on
\mathbb{R}
.

1.3.
Main results of this paper

We state the main results of the paper.
As in
[
29
]
, we first introduce function spaces for measuring the Sobolev regularity of hydroelastic waves.
A simplified model for (
1.7
) is given by its linearization around the zero solution:

(1.11)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\partial_{t}w+\partial_{\alpha}r=0,&\\
\partial_{t}r-i\partial^{4}_{\alpha}w=0,&\end{array}\right.

restricted to holomorphic functions.
(
1.11
) is a system of equations that can be written as a linear dispersive equation

\partial_{t}^{2}w+i\partial^{5}_{\alpha}w=0.

Its dispersion relation is given by

\tau^{2}+\xi^{5}=0,\quad\xi<0,

which is different from the dispersion relation of linearized gravity or capillary water waves.

A conserved energy associated with (
1.11
) is given by

(1.12)

\mathcal{E}_{0}(w,r)=\int-iw_{\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}+|r|^{2}\,d\alpha=\|w\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}}^{2}+\|r\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

This conserved energy suggests the functional framework to study (
1.7
).
The system (
1.11
) is well-posed in the product space
\dot{H}^{\frac{3}{2}}\times L^{2}
.
Motivated by the linearized analysis, we introduce inhomogeneous product Sobolev spaces and the corresponding inhomogeneous product Zygmund spaces:

\mathcal{H}^{s}:=H^{s+\frac{3}{2}}\times H^{s},\quad\mathcal{W}^{r}:=C^{r+\frac{3}{2}}_{*}\times C^{r}_{*}.

The hydroelastic wave system (
1.7
) is a quasilinear dispersive PDE system of order
\frac{5}{2}
.

Ambrose and Siegel
[
15
]
showed that the system (
1.5
) is locally well-posed in
\mathcal{H}^{n}
when
n
is large enough.
Wang and Yang obtained a refined result for hydroelastic waves in
[
52
]
showing that the system (
1.7
) is locally well-posed in
\mathcal{H}^{\frac{5}{2}k}
for
k>2
.
Since the flexural elasticity
\mathbf{E}(\eta)
is highly nonlinear and strongly geometry-dependent, they adopted the geometric framework developed by Shatah & Zeng
[
44
,
43
,
45
]
, formulating the problem as a dispersive equation for
\mathbf{E}(\eta)
.
This approach, while robust, necessitates relatively high regularity assumptions on the initial data.

To state the main results in this paper, we define the control norms that will be used in the energy estimate.
Let
0<\epsilon<\epsilon^{{}^{\prime}}\ll 1
be two positive constants, and
s\geq 0
. Define

\mathcal{A}_{s}:=\|(\mathbf{W},R)\|_{\mathcal{W}^{s-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}},\quad\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,s}:=\|(\mathbf{W},R)\|_{W^{s+\frac{1}{4}+\epsilon^{{}^{\prime}},4}(\mathbb{R})\times W^{s-\frac{5}{4}+\epsilon^{{}^{\prime}},4}(\mathbb{R})}.

Using Sobolev embedding,
\mathcal{A}_{s}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,s}
.
It is noted that
\mathcal{A}_{0}
is scale-invariant with respect to the scaling (
1.10
) up to the presence of the small parameter
\epsilon
.
The small constants
\epsilon
and
\epsilon^{{}^{\prime}}
are needed to avoid the delicate endpoint Sobolev estimates.

The first main result of this paper is an improved cubic energy estimate.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
(\mathbf{W},R)
solve the hydroelastic wave system (
1.7
).
For any
s>0
, there exists an energy functional
E_{s}(\mathbf{W},R)
that has the following properties:

(1)

Norm equivalence:

(1.13)

E_{s}(\mathbf{W},R)\approx_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(\mathbf{W},R)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}.

(2)

Improved energy estimates:

(1.14)

\frac{d}{dt}E_{s}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}E_{s}.

Remark 1.1
.

For comparison, in the cubic energy estimate in
[
55
]
, the constant in the energy estimate is
{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{5}{2}}
.
The Sobolev index
\frac{7}{4}
in (
1.14
) is much smaller than
\frac{5}{2}
.
In addition, Theorem
1.1
works for any positive index
s>0
, while the corresponding energy estimate in
[
55
]
only states the result for
s=n+\frac{1}{2}
, where
n\geq 2
is an integer.

Remark 1.2
.

When the gravity is taken into account, one can prove almost the same result as Theorem
1.1
.
The only difference is that in (
1.14
), the constant
{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}
is replaced by
g+{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}
.
This is because the gravity term is of lower order than the elastic terms and does not play a significant role in the analysis.

The second main result of this paper is the local well-posedness result of the hydroelastic wave system (
1.7
).

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
s>\frac{3}{4}
, the system (
1.7
) is locally well-posed in
{\mathcal{H}}^{s}(\mathbb{R})

(\text{or }\,{\mathcal{H}}^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
.
Moreover, the solutions of (
1.7
) exist on
[0,T]
as long as
\mathcal{A}_{0}(t)\lesssim 1
and
{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}(t)\in L^{2}([0,T])
.

Well-posedness is understood in the sense of Hadamard, namely existence, uniqueness, and continuous dependence on initial data.

We also address the related work of Alazard, Kukavica & Tuffaha
[
11
]
and Alazard, Shao & Yang
[
12
]
.
They proved the global existence of water waves where the interface evolution is governed by the law of linear elasticity.
The equation in their model can be reduced to a Schrödinger type equation whose leading dispersive term has a constant coefficient.
In our model, the dynamic boundary condition (
1.4
) with nonlinear elasticity is different.
The hydroelastic waves (
1.7
) can be rewritten as a quasilinear dispersive equation of order
\frac{5}{2}
instead of a Schrödinger type equation.
Consequently, both the structure of the governing equations and the analytical methods employed here differ substantially from those in
[
11
,
12
]
.

1.4.
Key Difficulties and Strategy of Proof

The primary challenge in establishing the low-regularity well-posedness for the hydroelastic wave system (
1.7
) lies in its quasilinear nature and the high order of the dispersive term.
The system is a dispersive equation of order
\frac{5}{2}
.
In standard Sobolev spaces, the nonlinearity induces a “loss of derivatives” in the energy estimates, where the time derivative of the energy norm is controlled only by norms of higher order.
To overcome this, we employ a strategy based on paradifferential calculus and the method of modified energy, inspired by the works of Hunter-Ifrim-Tataru
[
26
]
, and Ai-Ifrim-Tataru
[
3
]
on gravity water waves.

1. Quasilinear Structure and Holomorphic Coordinates.
We work in holomorphic coordinates, which effectively diagonalizes the system and simplifies the structure of the Dirichlet-Neumann operator
G(\eta)
.
However, the resulting system (
1.7
) remains quasilinear.
Our first step is to paralinearize the equations.
By decomposing the nonlinearity into paradifferential operators (low-high interactions) and remainders (high-high interactions), we isolate the principal transport and dispersive terms.
Roughly speaking, this reduction allows us to treat the equations as a linear dispersive system with rough, variable coefficients, plus perturbative source terms. Notably, the highly nonlinear nature of the elastic term
\mathbf{E}(\eta)
makes this step particularly intricate and subtle compared to the case of gravity/capillary water waves.

2. The Modified Energy Functional.

Standard energy estimates are insufficient due to the variable coefficients in the leading-order terms.
A crucial component of our proof is the construction of a modified energy functional
E_{s}(\mathbf{W},R)
in Theorem
1.1
and the linearized modified energy
E^{s,para}_{lin}(w,r)
in Proposition
4.2
.
Unlike the standard
H^{s}
energies, the principal part of our functional includes a carefully chosen paradifferential weight that depends on the Jacobian
J
and the parameter
s
.
Specifically, for the linearized variables
(w,r)
, we construct the principal part of the modified energy of the form in Section
3.1
:

E_{lin}(w,r)\approx\int\Im\left(T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\right)+\Re\left(r\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}\bar{r}\right)\,d\alpha.

The specific powers of the Jacobian
J^{-\frac{5}{4}}
and
J^{\frac{1}{4}}
are critical.
They are selected to ensure that the leading-order contributions from the time derivatives of the para-coefficients cancel out exactly with the leading-order non-perturbative terms arising from the elastic nonlinearity. This cancellation is essential to close the energy estimates without losing regularity.

3. Normal Forms and Low Regularity.

To reach the low regularity threshold
s>3/4
, we must control cubic and quartic nonlinear interactions that essentially behave as perturbative source terms in the energy estimate.
We employ paradifferential normal form transformations to eliminate the non-perturbative portions of these terms.
We define a transformation
(\mathbf{W},R)\mapsto(\mathbf{W}_{NF},R_{NF})
such that the new variables satisfy a “better” paradifferential system where the cubic nonlinearities are either null forms or have favorable derivative structures.
The feasibility of this normal form method relies on the non-resonant structure of the hydroelastic dispersion relation (
C.1
).
As discussed in Appendix
C
, the strict convexity of the function
|\xi|^{\frac{5}{2}}
ensures that three-wave resonances cannot occur for nonzero frequencies, and four-wave resonances are avoided in the relevant interaction regimes.
This allows us to define bounded bilinear and trilinear symbols that remove the problematic terms from the energy inequality.

The remainder of this paper is organized as follows.
Section
2
is devoted to the computation of para-material derivatives for both the full hydroelastic waves and the linearized hydroelastic waves.
In Section
3
, we derive the modified energy estimate of the linearized hydroelastic waves (
2.5
), namely Theorem
3.1
.
Then, in Section
4
, we prove the modified energy estimate for the full hydroelastic waves Theorem
1.1
.
We briefly explain how to obtain the low regularity well-posedness of two-dimensional deep hydroelastic waves following the argument in
[
3
]
in Section
5
.
As for the appendix, we put the paradifferential estimates that we will need in this paper in Appendix
A
.
We will rewrite different variants of hydroelastic waves in the paradifferential format, so that these paradifferential estimates play a crucial role.
In Appendix
B
, we recall some estimates for several auxiliary functions in Sobolev and Zygmund spaces.
Finally, in Appendix
C
, we discuss three-wave and four-wave interactions.
We will perform paradifferential quadratic/cubic normal forms and construct cubic/quartic modified energy later in the analysis, which require appropriate non-resonant conditions.

2.
Para-material derivatives and the linearized hydroelastic waves

In holomorphic coordinates, the material derivative is defined to be
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+b\partial_{\alpha}
.
At the paradifferential level, it is replaced by the para-material derivative
T_{D_{t}}=\partial_{t}+T_{b}\partial_{\alpha}
.
In this section, we compute the leading contributions to the para-material derivatives of variables
(\mathbf{W},R)
and the weight
J^{s}
.
In addition, we compute and simplify the linearized hydroelastic wave system, and identify the principal terms of para-material derivatives of linearized variables
(w,r)
.
These formulas will play a key role in estimating the time derivative of the modified energy in later sections.
We rewrite each equation as the paradifferential equation.
By carefully separating principal contributions from lower-order terms, and by treating all perturbative components as error terms, we obtain the leading terms of para-material derivatives of each variable.

2.1.
Leading terms of para-material derivatives

We first compute the leading terms of the para-material derivatives of
\mathbf{W}
.

Lemma 2.1
.

The unknown
\mathbf{W}
satisfies the paradifferential equation:

(2.1)

T_{D_{t}}\mathbf{W}+T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}R-T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}R=G,

where the source term
G
satisfies the bound

\|G\|_{C_{*}^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}},\quad\|G\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}.

Proof.

We rewrite the first equation in (
1.7
) in the form of paradifferential equations

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}\mathbf{W}+T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}+T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-T_{1+\mathbf{W}}T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}R

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})}-T_{1+\mathbf{W}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\right)R_{\alpha}-T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathbf{W}+\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{Y},R)-\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\mathbf{W},b).

Using (
A.5
), (
B.2
), (
A.8
) and (
A.13
) to estimate the Zygmund bound of the right-hand side of above equation, we write

\displaystyle\|(T_{1+\mathbf{W}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}-T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})})R_{\alpha}\|_{C_{*}^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{7}{4}}\|R\|_{C_{*}^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}},

\displaystyle\|T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathbf{W}\|_{C_{*}^{1}}\lesssim\|b\|_{C^{\frac{1}{4}}_{*}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{C_{*}^{\frac{7}{4}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{Y},R)\|_{C_{*}^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|Y\|_{C^{\frac{7}{4}}_{*}}\|R\|_{C^{\frac{1}{4}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\mathbf{W},b)\|_{C_{*}^{1}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{C^{\frac{7}{4}}_{*}}\|b\|_{C^{\frac{1}{4}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}}.

Similarly, for Sobolev bound, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(T_{1+\mathbf{W}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}-T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})})R_{\alpha}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}\|R\|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}},

\displaystyle\|T_{b_{\alpha}}\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim\|b\|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{W^{2,4}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\alpha}T_{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{Y},R)\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|Y\|_{W^{2,4}}\|R\|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\mathbf{W},b)\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{W^{2,4}}\|b\|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}.

Furthermore, applying the symbolic calculus rules (
A.3
) and (
A.5
) gives

T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-T_{1+\mathbf{W}}T_{\bar{Y}_{\alpha}}R=T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}R-T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}R+G.

This leads to the equation for
T_{D_{t}}\mathbf{W}
(
2.1
).
∎

We note that estimating the error term in
L^{\infty}
norm, then

\mathbf{W}_{t}=-T_{(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}+G,\quad\|G\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}}.

Next, we compute the leading terms of the para-material derivatives of
R
.

Lemma 2.2
.

The leading term of the para-material derivative of
R
is given by

(2.2)

T_{D_{t}}R=iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{15}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+K,

and the error term
K
satisfies the estimate

\|K\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}.

Proof.

The second equation of the system (
1.7
) can be rewritten as

(2.3)

T_{D_{t}}R=-i{\mathbf{P}}[a(1-Y)]-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(R_{\alpha},b)-{\mathbf{P}}T_{R_{\alpha}}b+\text{elastic terms},

where the elastic terms are given by

\displaystyle i(1-Y){\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}\{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}[J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})]\}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle i(1-Y){\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}\{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}[J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})]\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}(1-Y){\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{3}-3J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}(1-Y){\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{3}-3J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}].

For the non-elastic terms on the right-hand side of (
2.3
), we compute using (
B.1
)

\displaystyle\|a(1-Y)\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim(1+\|Y\|_{L^{\infty}})\|a\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(R_{\alpha},b)\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}T_{R_{\alpha}}b\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|R\|_{W^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon,4}}\|b\|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}},

so that these three terms can be absorbed into
K
.
It suffices to simplify these elastic terms.

We compute

J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})=J^{-1}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}-\frac{3}{2}J^{-1}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}J^{-2}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2},

so that we get

\displaystyle J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}[J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-2J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}-J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}

\displaystyle-3J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}+\frac{9}{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{3}+\frac{3}{2}J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}.

We then use the above computation to expand the elastic terms,

\displaystyle\text{Elastic terms}=

\displaystyle i(1-Y){\mathbf{P}}\partial_{\alpha}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-5J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}

\displaystyle-J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}+5J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{3}-J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}

\displaystyle+5J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}+J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}-5J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{3}].

Putting perturbative terms into
K
, we get

\displaystyle\text{Elastic terms}=

\displaystyle i(1-Y)\Big[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}

\displaystyle-5T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}\Big]+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iT_{1-Y}\Big[T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}-\frac{15}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}\Big]+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{15}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+K.

Collecting the expressions of all terms in the equation (
2.3
), we obtain the principal part of
T_{D_{t}}R
in (
2.2
).
∎

We now turn to the computation of the time derivative of the weight
J^{s}
for
s\neq 0
.

Lemma 2.3
.

The time derivative of
J^{s}
satisfies

\partial_{t}J^{s}=-sJ^{s}b_{\alpha}+E=-sT_{J^{s}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}-sT_{J^{s}(1-Y)}\bar{R}_{\alpha}+E,\quad\|E\|_{C_{*}^{\epsilon}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}}.

Proof.

A direct computation yields

\partial_{t}J^{s}=sJ^{s}(1-Y)\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}+sJ^{s}(1-\bar{Y})\partial_{t}\bar{\mathbf{W}}.

The first equation of (
1.7
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}=

\displaystyle-(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}-b\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+(1+\mathbf{W})M

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(1+\mathbf{W})(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+E,

where, as in the derivation of (
2.1
), the terms
b\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+(1+\mathbf{W})M
are absorbed into the error term
E
.
As a result of the identity
(1+\mathbf{W})(1-Y)=1
, we have

\partial_{t}J^{s}=-sJ^{s}[(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}+(1-Y)\bar{R}_{\alpha}]+E=-sT_{J^{s}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}-sT_{J^{s}(1-Y)}\bar{R}_{\alpha}+E.

Finally, invoking identity (
1.9
) together with estimate (
B.6
) for
M
, we obtain

\partial_{t}J^{s}=-sJ^{s}(b_{\alpha}+M)+E=-sJ^{s}b_{\alpha}+E,

which completes the proof for
s\neq 0
.
∎

2.2.
The derivation of the linearized hydroelastic waves

In this section, we compute the linearized hydroelastic wave system.
Following the framework in gravity water waves
[
26
]
and capillary water waves
[
29
]
, we denote the solutions for the linearized hydroelastic waves around a solution
(W,Q)
to the equation (
1.5
) by
(w,q)
, and perturbative terms
(G,K)
for source terms that satisfy

\|(G,K)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

In what follows, we will repeatedly employ the symbolic calculus (
A.3
) to combine or exchange para-coefficients.
We will also use para-associativity Lemma
A.5
to commute the para-coefficients and the balanced paraproducts.
These computations are performed without explicit exposition, and commutator errors are absorbed into the perturbative source terms
(G,K)
.

The linearizations of non-elastic terms are the same as in the capillary water waves
[
29
]
.
To compute the linearizations of the elastic terms, we first introduce the auxiliary functions
\tilde{c}
and
c
by

i\tilde{c}:=\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})},\quad ic:=iJ^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{c}_{\alpha})+\frac{1}{2}(i\tilde{c})^{3}.

A direct computation shows that the linearization of
J^{-\frac{1}{2}}
is

\delta(J^{-\frac{1}{2}})=-\frac{(1+\mathbf{W})\bar{w}_{\alpha}+(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})w_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

We then obtain the linearization of
ic
, which is
i\delta c=p-\bar{p}
, where

\displaystyle p:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\left(\frac{3\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)w_{\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})w_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})w_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\left(\frac{3\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)w_{\alpha}\right)\left[\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right]^{2}.

By combining the above computations with those in
[
29
]
, the linearized hydroelastic waves are as follows:

(2.4)

\begin{cases}w_{t}+Fw_{\alpha}+(1+\mathbf{W}){\mathbf{P}}[m-\bar{m}]=0,\\
q_{t}+Fq_{\alpha}+Q_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}[m-\bar{m}]+{\mathbf{P}}[n+\bar{n}]-i{\mathbf{P}}[p-\bar{p}]=0.\end{cases}

Here, we introduce the diagonal linearized variable
r:=q-Rw
, and define

\displaystyle m:=\frac{q_{\alpha}-Rw_{\alpha}}{J}+\frac{\bar{R}w_{\alpha}}{(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}=\frac{r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w}{J}+\frac{\bar{R}w_{\alpha}}{(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}},

\displaystyle n:=\bar{R}\delta R=\frac{\bar{R}(q_{\alpha}-Rw_{\alpha})}{1+\mathbf{W}}=\frac{\bar{R}(r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w)}{1+\mathbf{W}}.

Introducing the transport coefficient
b=F+\frac{\bar{R}}{1+\mathbf{W}}
, and writing the equations in terms of the associated material derivative, the system (
2.4
) may be rewritten as

(2.5)

\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}+b\partial_{\alpha})w+\frac{1}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}r_{\alpha}+\frac{R_{\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}w=\mathcal{G}_{0}(w,r),\\
(\partial_{t}+b\partial_{\alpha})r-i\frac{a}{1+\mathbf{W}}w-i{\mathbf{P}}p-\frac{w}{1+\mathbf{W}}\partial_{\alpha}{\mathbf{P}}c=\mathcal{K}_{0}(w,r),\end{cases}

where on the right-hand side,

\mathcal{G}_{0}(w,r)=(1+\mathbf{W})({\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}+\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}m),\quad\mathcal{K}_{0}(w,r)=\bar{{\mathbf{P}}}n-{\mathbf{P}}\bar{n}-i{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}.

In the remainder of the paper, we devote substantial effort to the analysis of the linearized system (
2.5
) and various variants of (
2.5
).
In order to obtain a modified energy estimate for (
2.5
) at low regularity, we will first rewrite the system in paradifferential form.

The reduction of (
2.5
) to a paradifferential system follows closely the corresponding computation for gravity water waves
[
3
]
.
The principal new difficulty lies in the elastic contribution
-i{\mathbf{P}}p
, whose structure requires additional analysis.
To facilitate the computation, we first prove the following lemma.

Lemma 2.4
.

The term
(1+\mathbf{W})p
admits the following decomposition:

(2.6)

\begin{split}(1+\mathbf{W})p=&\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})))\\
&-i\left[\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}\right)\right)+\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}\right)\right)\right]\\
&-\frac{11}{4}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}^{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}\right)-icw_{\alpha}+K.\end{split}

Proof.

Recall that
p
is given by

\displaystyle p:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\left(\frac{3\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}-\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)w_{\alpha}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})w_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})w_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left[\frac{1}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}}\frac{d}{d\alpha}\left(\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\left(\frac{3\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)w_{\alpha}\right)\left[\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\right]^{2},

which we decompose as

p:=A-B-C+D.

A direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\frac{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})}-\left(\frac{3\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}-\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}{2J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)w_{\alpha}=\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})-i\tilde{c}w_{\alpha}}{1+\mathbf{W}}=:\frac{A_{0}}{1+\mathbf{W}}.

A straightforward calculation gives that

\displaystyle J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{A_{0}}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right)=\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0}}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right)-\frac{\partial_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0}}{1+\mathbf{W}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0}\right)}{1+\mathbf{W}}-\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0}}{(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}-\frac{\partial_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0}}{1+\mathbf{W}}=\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0})-\frac{i\tilde{c}A_{0}}{2}}{1+\mathbf{W}}=:\frac{A_{1}}{1+\mathbf{W}}.

Replace
A_{0}
by
A_{1}
in the above equations, we then get

\displaystyle A=

\displaystyle J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{A_{1}}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right)=\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{1})-\frac{i\tilde{c}A_{1}}{2}}{1+\mathbf{W}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\mathbf{W}}\left(\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0}))-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\left((i\tilde{c})J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0}\right)-\frac{i\tilde{c}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}A_{0})}{2}+\frac{(i\tilde{c})^{2}A_{0}}{4}\right).

Similarly,
B
is given by

\displaystyle B=\frac{1}{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\frac{J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}w_{\alpha}}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}w_{\alpha})-\frac{i\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}w_{\alpha}}{2}}{1+\mathbf{W}}.

The term
C
is

\displaystyle C=\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{1+\mathbf{W}}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(ic)_{\alpha}\right)w_{\alpha}.

For
D
, we have

\displaystyle D=\frac{3}{2}\frac{A_{0}}{1+\mathbf{W}}(i\tilde{c})^{2}.

In the following, we will simplify terms in
(1+\mathbf{W})p
according to the number of derivatives that may act on
w
.

Fourth-order term of
w
in
(1+\mathbf{W})p
:

\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}))).

Third-order terms of
w
in
(1+\mathbf{W})p
:

\displaystyle-\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})w_{\alpha}))-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\left((i\tilde{c})J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})\right)-\frac{i\tilde{c}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}))}{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})w_{\alpha}))-\partial_{\alpha}\left((i\tilde{c})J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})\right)+\frac{(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})}{2}.

Second-order terms of
w
in
(1+\mathbf{W})p
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}\left((i\tilde{c})^{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}\right)+\frac{i\tilde{c}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})w_{\alpha})}{2}+\frac{(i\tilde{c})^{2}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})}{4}

\displaystyle+\frac{3(i\tilde{c})^{2}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})}{2}+\frac{(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha})}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}w_{\alpha})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{11}{4}\partial_{\alpha}\left((i\tilde{c})^{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}\right)-4(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})w_{\alpha})-\frac{1}{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha})w_{\alpha}.

First-order terms of
w
in
(1+\mathbf{W})p
:

\displaystyle-4(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})w_{\alpha})+\frac{1}{4}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})w_{\alpha})-\frac{1}{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha})w_{\alpha}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(i\tilde{c})_{\alpha})w_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{4}(i\tilde{c})^{3}w_{\alpha}-\frac{3}{2}(i\tilde{c})^{3}w_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-icw_{\alpha}+\frac{15}{4}\tilde{c}_{\alpha}\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}-\frac{5i}{4}\tilde{c}^{3}w_{\alpha}.

Note that

\left\|\frac{15}{4}\tilde{c}_{\alpha}\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}w_{\alpha}-\frac{5i}{4}\tilde{c}^{3}w_{\alpha}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}},

so that this term can be put into the perturbative source term
K
.
Collecting the contributions at each derivative order in
(1+\mathbf{W})p
, we obtain the expansion in (
2.6
).
∎

Motivated by the expansion of
(1+\mathbf{W})p
in (
2.6
), we introduce the operator

\begin{split}L:=&\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha})))-i\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\right)\right)\\
&-i\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\right)\right)-\frac{11}{4}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}^{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\right)-ic\partial_{\alpha}-i{{\mathbf{P}}}c_{\alpha}.\end{split}

Then the system becomes

\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}+b\partial_{\alpha})w+\frac{1}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}r_{\alpha}+\frac{R_{\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}w=\mathcal{G}_{0}(w,r),\\
(\partial_{t}+b\partial_{\alpha})r-i\frac{a}{1+\mathbf{W}}w-i{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{Lw}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right]=\mathcal{K}_{0}(w,r)+K.\end{cases}

Applying the projection
{\mathbf{P}}
and using
\mathfrak{M}_{b}f:={\mathbf{P}}[bf]
, the linearized hydroelastic waves can be rewritten as

\begin{cases}(\partial_{t}+\mathfrak{M}_{b}\partial_{\alpha})w+{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{1}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}r_{\alpha}\right]+{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{R_{\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}w\right]={\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},\\
(\partial_{t}+\mathfrak{M}_{b}\partial_{\alpha})r-i{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{a}{1+\mathbf{W}}w\right]-i{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{Lw}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right]={\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}+K.\end{cases}

In the following, we compute and simplify the term
-i{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{Lw}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right]
.

\begin{split}{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)Lw]=&{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w]+{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)\left[2\partial_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-2i\tilde{c}J^{-1}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w\right\}\\
+&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)\left[2J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\alpha}J^{-1}+3J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{2}-3\partial_{\alpha}(i\tilde{c}J^{-1})-\frac{11}{4}\tilde{c}^{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w\right\}\\
+&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)[J^{-1}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}})-2iJ^{-1}\tilde{c}_{\alpha\alpha}]w_{\alpha}\right\}-i{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\tilde{c}w\right]+K.\end{split}

We now simplify each term in
{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)Lw]
successively.
For the first term

{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w]=T_{(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w+T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w}{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1]+{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}w,(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1).

Using the paralinearization in Lemma
A.6
, we can write

(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1=-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbf{W}-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}+err,\quad\|err\|_{C_{*}^{\frac{7}{2}}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w]=

\displaystyle T_{(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w}\mathbf{W}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,\mathbf{W})-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}})+K.

For the second term in
{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)Lw]
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)\left[2\partial_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-2i\tilde{c}J^{-1}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w\right\}\\
=&-{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[5(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+(1-\bar{Y})\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w\right\}\\
=&-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-5T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w}{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\right]\\
&-T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w}{\mathbf{P}}\left[J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\right]-5{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})\,\\
=&-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\\
&-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})+K\,.\end{split}

For the third term in
{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)Lw]
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)\left[2J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\alpha}J^{-1}+3J^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\partial_{\alpha}J^{-\frac{1}{2}})^{2}-3\partial_{\alpha}(i\tilde{c}J^{-1})-\frac{11}{4}\tilde{c}^{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w\right\}\\
=&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\frac{(1-Y)}{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left[-\frac{5\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}{1+\mathbf{W}}+\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}+15\frac{\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}}{(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{2}w\right\}\\
=&-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}+T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}+15T_{(1-Y)^{3}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}\\
&-5T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}\right]+T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}\right]+15T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-Y)^{3}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}\right]\\
&-5{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})+{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})\\
&+15{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},(1-Y)^{3}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha})\,\\
=&-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}+T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}+15T_{(1-Y)^{3}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}\\
&-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})+T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})+K.\end{split}

Next, for the fourth term in
{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)Lw]
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)[J^{-1}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}})-2iJ^{-1}\tilde{c}_{\alpha\alpha}]w_{\alpha}\right\}\\
=&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[-\frac{5}{2}\frac{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}{1+\mathbf{W}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\right]w_{\alpha}\,\right\}+K\\
=&-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}w_{\alpha}+\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}w_{\alpha}-\frac{5}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}\right]\\
&+\frac{3}{2}T_{w_{\alpha}}{\mathbf{P}}\left[J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}\right]-\frac{5}{2}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})+\frac{3}{2}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}})+K\,\\
=&-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}w_{\alpha}+\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}w_{\alpha}-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}\\
&-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})+\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}})+K.\end{split}

Finally, for the last term in
{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)Lw]
,

\begin{split}&-{\mathbf{P}}\left[(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}{\mathbf{P}}(i\tilde{c})w\right]\\
=&-T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}w-T_{w}{\mathbf{P}}[(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}]\\
&-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi\left(w,(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}\right)+{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w,J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}})+K\,\\
=&-T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}w-T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi\left(w,\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}\right)+K.\end{split}

Gathering the computations for all terms for
-i{\mathbf{P}}\left[\frac{Lw}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right]
, one can rewrite the linearized system in the
paradifferential form as follows:

(2.7)

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}w+T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}w=\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}(w,r)+G,\\
T_{D_{t}}r-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w=\mathcal{K}^{\sharp}(w,r)+K,\end{cases}

where the operator
\mathcal{L}_{para}
, which can be viewed as the paradifferential version of the operator
L
, is given by

(2.8)

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{para}w=&T_{(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-5T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}\\
&+T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}+15T_{(1-Y)^{3}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}w_{\alpha}\\
&+\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}w_{\alpha}-T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}w,\end{split}

and source terms
\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}={\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{G}_{0}+\mathcal{G}_{1})
,
\mathcal{K}^{\sharp}={\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}+\mathcal{K}_{1})
with

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}=

\displaystyle\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{Y})-(T_{w_{\alpha}}b+\Pi(w_{\alpha},b))-T_{w}((1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha})-\Pi(w,(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}=

\displaystyle-T_{r_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R-\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R)-\Pi(r_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\bar{R})

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,\mathbf{W})-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}})

\displaystyle-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}})

\displaystyle-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w,\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}).

2.3.
Quadratic bounds for paradifferential source terms

In this section, we compute the leading part of source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
,
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
, and use the results to obtain the leading terms of
(T_{D_{t}}w,T_{D_{t}}r)
.

We first simplify source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
, which are given by the following results.

Lemma 2.5
.

The source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}=

\displaystyle-T_{J^{-1}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}\mathbf{W}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R-T_{J^{-1}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+G,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}R+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy

(2.9)

\|(G,K)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

Proof.

We first consider the estimate for the source term
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}
.
The auxiliary linearized variables
m
can be written as

m=|1-Y|^{2}(r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w)+(1-Y)^{2}\bar{R}w_{\alpha},

so that by putting perturbative terms into
G
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}=-{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})(\bar{r}_{\alpha}+\bar{R}_{\alpha}\bar{w})Y]+{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{w}_{\alpha}R]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{(1-\bar{Y})(\bar{r}_{\alpha}+\bar{R}_{\alpha}\bar{w})}Y-{\mathbf{P}}\Pi((1-\bar{Y})(\bar{r}_{\alpha}+\bar{R}_{\alpha}\bar{w}),Y)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R+{\mathbf{P}}\Pi((1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}Y-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R-T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+G.

By using the paralinearization result of
Y
(
B.5
),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}=T_{1+\mathbf{W}}{\mathbf{P}}\bar{m}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{J^{-1}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}\mathbf{W}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R-T_{J^{-1}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+G.

Next, we consider the source term
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}
.
The auxiliary linearized function is
n=(1-Y)\bar{R}(r_{\alpha}+R_{\alpha}w)
, which gives

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\bar{n}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})\bar{r}_{\alpha}R]+{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})\bar{R}_{\alpha}\bar{w}R]={\mathbf{P}}[T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{r}_{\alpha}R]+K

\displaystyle=T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}R+T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)+K.

It remains to simplify the
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}
term.
According to the computation in (
2.6
),

\displaystyle\bar{p}=

\displaystyle(1-\bar{Y})\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha})))

\displaystyle+i\left[\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\right)\right)+\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\left(J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{11}{4}\partial_{\alpha}\left(\tilde{c}^{2}J^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\right)+ic\bar{w}_{\alpha}+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\bar{Y})J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}-(1-\bar{Y})J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[5(1-\bar{Y})\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}

\displaystyle+\frac{(1-\bar{Y})}{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left[-\frac{5\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}+\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}{1+\mathbf{W}}+15\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{2}_{\alpha}}{(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})^{2}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\bar{w}

\displaystyle+(1-\bar{Y})J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[-\frac{5}{2}\frac{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right]\bar{w}_{\alpha}+K

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\,\bar{p}_{1}+\bar{p}_{2}+\bar{p}_{3}+\bar{p}_{4}+K.

For
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p_{1}}
, we have
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{1}={\mathbf{P}}[((1-\bar{Y})J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1)\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}].

Using the paralinearization in Lemma
A.6
, we can write

(1-\bar{Y})J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1=-\frac{5}{2}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\mathbf{W}+err,\quad\|err\|_{C_{*}^{\frac{7}{2}}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}^{2}_{\frac{7}{4}}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{1}=-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{W}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w})+K=-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}-\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W})+K.

For
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{2}
, we have

\begin{split}{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{2}=&-{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-\bar{Y})J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[5(1-\bar{Y})\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}+(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\right\}\\
=&-5{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}]-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}).\end{split}

Applying the commutator estimate, the first term of
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{2}
is perturbative:

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}[J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}]\|_{L^{2}}=\|[{\mathbf{P}},J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}]\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{P}}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}\|_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}_{*}}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\|T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}(J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1)\|_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}_{*}}+\|{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}},J^{-\frac{3}{2}}-1)\|_{C^{\frac{3}{2}}_{*}}\right)\|w\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\|w\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Therefore, we obtain

\begin{split}{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{2}=&-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w})+K=-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}-T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})+K.\end{split}

Similarly, for the rest two terms, by absorbing perturbative terms into
K
,

\begin{split}{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{3}=&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{\frac{(1-\bar{Y})}{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left[-\frac{5\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}+\frac{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}{1+\mathbf{W}}+15\frac{\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{2}_{\alpha}}{(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})^{2}}\right]\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\bar{w}\right\}\\
=&T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha})+K=T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}+T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})+K,\\
{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}_{4}=&{\mathbf{P}}\left\{(1-\bar{Y})J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\left[-\frac{5}{2}\frac{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}{1+\bar{\mathbf{W}}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}{1+\mathbf{W}}\right]\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,\right\}\\
=&\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}\bar{w}_{\alpha})+K=\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+\frac{3}{2}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})+K.\end{split}

Collecting the above, we finally obtain

\displaystyle i{\mathbf{P}}\bar{p}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})+K.

Putting each term in
{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}
yields the expression for its leading part.
∎

Then we simplify source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.
By putting the perturbative terms into
(G,K)
and commuting para-coefficients, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 2.6
.

The source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}

\displaystyle=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}R-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w_{\alpha},R)-T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{R})+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{\mathbf{W}})+G,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}

\displaystyle=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{r_{\alpha}}R-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w}\mathbf{W}-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},R)-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w,\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})-T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{R})-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}})+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
2.9
).

Finally, after obtaining the source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
and
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
, we compute the leading terms of para-material derivatives of
(w,r)
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Suppose
(w,r)
solve the paradifferential linearized equations (
2.7
), then we have the expressions for
(T_{D_{t}}w,T_{D_{t}}r)
.

(2.10)

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}w=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}(r_{\alpha}+T_{w}R_{\alpha}+T_{w_{\alpha}}R)+\tilde{G}+G,

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}r=

\displaystyle iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\big(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w-5T_{1-Y}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}T_{w}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}T_{1-Y}T_{w_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{3}{2}T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}\big)+\tilde{K}+K,

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
2.9
), and
(\tilde{G},\tilde{K})
satisfy the estimate

\|(\tilde{G},\tilde{K})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}.

Proof.

A direct computation for each part of the paradifferential equation (
2.7
) yields

\displaystyle T_{(1-\bar{Y})R_{\alpha}}w=T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w+G=\tilde{G}+G,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}=\tilde{G}+G,\quad{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}T_{w}R+\tilde{G}+G,

\displaystyle i\mathcal{L}_{para}w=iT_{(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w+\tilde{K}+K,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}=-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+\tilde{K}+K,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}=-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+\tilde{K}+K.

By adding each non-perturbative part of the paradifferential equation (
2.7
) together and rewriting para-coefficients, we obtain the leading terms of
(T_{D_{t}}w,T_{D_{t}}r)
.
∎

3.
Energy estimates for the linearized hydroelastic waves

This section is dedicated to constructing the modified energy and establishing the modified energy estimate for the linearized hydroelastic system (
2.5
).
Specifically, we prove the following theorem.

Theorem 3.1
.

Assume that
\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\lesssim 1
and
\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}\in L^{2}_{t}([0,T])
.
Then the linearized hydroelastic system (
2.5
) is well-posed in
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}
on
[0,T]
.
Furthermore, there exists an energy functional
E^{0}_{lin}(w,r)
satisfying the following properties on
[0,T]
:

(1)

Norm equivalence:

E^{0}_{lin}(w,r)=\left(1+O\left(\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\right)\right)\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

(2)

The time derivative of
E_{lin}^{0}(w,r)
is bounded by:

\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}^{0}(w,r)\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}E^{0}_{lin}(w,r).

The proof of Theorem
3.1
is divided into two main steps.
First, we consider the linear part of paradifferential linearized hydroelastic waves (
2.7
) with perturbative source terms, namely, the system:

(3.1)

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}w+T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w=G,\\
T_{D_{t}}r-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w=K,\end{cases}

where the operator
\mathcal{L}_{para}
is defined in (
2.8
), and
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
2.9
).
We establish the following modified energy estimate for this system.

Proposition 3.2
.

Assume that
\mathcal{A}_{0}\lesssim 1
and
\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}\in L^{2}_{t}([0,T])
for some time
T>0
, then if
(w,r)
solve the homogeneous paradifferential system (
3.1
) on
[0,T]
, there exists an energy functional
E^{0,para}_{lin}(w,r)
such that on
[0,T]
, we have the following two properties:

(1)

Norm equivalence:

E^{0,para}_{lin}(w,r)=(1+O({\mathcal{A}_{0}}))\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

(2)

The time derivative of
E^{0,para}_{lin}(w,r)
is bounded by

\frac{d}{dt}E^{0,para}_{lin}(w,r)\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

To prove Proposition
3.2
, we first construct a quadratic paradifferential linearized energy
E_{lin}(w,r)
in (
3.3
).
This energy is designed to cancel the leading terms of
(T_{D_{t}}w,T_{D_{t}}r)
in the integral.
The time derivative
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}
also contains non-perturbative cubic energy, which we eliminate by constructing a cubic energy correction
E^{3}_{cor}
.
The remaining non-perturbative terms are subsequently removed by quartic energy corrections, so that
E^{0,para}_{lin}
defined in (
3.5
) is exactly the energy that we need in Proposition
3.2
.

In the second step, we address the non-perturbative source terms
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
.
Since
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
does not satisfy (
2.9
), Theorem
3.1
cannot follow directly from Proposition
3.2
.
We will construct linearized normal form variables

(w_{NF},r_{NF}):=(w_{NF}^{0},r_{NF}^{0})+(w_{NF}^{1},r_{NF}^{1})

to eliminate
(\mathcal{G}^{\sharp},\mathcal{K}^{\sharp})
.
For each part of the normal form variables, we will perform the normal form analysis for the low-high quadratic portion, balanced quadratic portion and cubic portion of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
or
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.
The normal form variables satisfy the bound

(3.2)

\|(w_{NF},r_{NF})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}},

and the pair
(w+w_{NF},r+r_{NF})
solves the paradifferential linearized flow (
3.1
) with perturbative source terms.
Choosing the modified energy

E^{0}_{lin}(w,r):=E^{0,para}_{lin}(w+w_{NF},r+r_{NF}),

and applying Proposition
3.2
and (
3.2
),
the modified energy
E^{0}_{lin}(w,r)
satisfies both the norm equivalence and cubic energy estimate in Theorem
3.1
.
The well-posedness of the linearized hydroelastic waves (
2.5
) follows from a standard fixed-point argument using the modified energy estimate.
This concludes the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

The remainder of this section focuses on proving the modified energy estimate for (
3.1
) and constructing normal form variables
(w_{NF},r_{NF})
.
In Section
3.1
, we prove Proposition
3.2
.
Then, in Section
3.2
, we compute
(w_{NF}^{0},r_{NF}^{0})
to eliminate
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
.
Finally, in Section
3.3
, we compute
(w_{NF}^{1},r_{NF}^{1})
to eliminate
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.

3.1.
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}
energy estimate of the homogeneous paradifferential flow

In this subsection, we construct the energy
E^{0,para}_{lin}(w,r)
in Proposition
3.2
, i.e. the
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}
modified energy estimate of the linear paradifferential flow (
3.1
).
We begin with a paradifferential linearized quadratic energy
E_{lin}(w,r)=(1+O({\mathcal{A}_{0}}))\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}
, and then construct cubic and quartic energy correction
E_{cor}(w,r)
such that its time derivative eliminates the non-perturbative part of
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
.

We define the paradifferential linearized energy
E_{lin}(w,r)
as

(3.3)

E_{lin}(w,r)=\int\Im\left(T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\right)+\Re\left(r\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}\bar{r}\right)+\Re(w\cdot\bar{w})\,d\alpha.

The para-coefficients
T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}
,
T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}
are selected for two reasons:

(1)

Cancellation of Leading Terms: The identity
J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})=J^{\frac{1}{4}}\cdot J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})
ensures that integral terms of the type
\Re\int ir\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}\,d\alpha
in
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
vanish due to the cancellation of para-coefficients.

(2)

Matching Coefficients for sub-leading terms: The exponents
-\frac{5}{4}
and
\frac{1}{4}
are chosen so that in
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
, integrals of the type
\Re\int ir\cdot T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha
and
\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w\,d\alpha
have the same coefficient. This leads to a cancellation when computing the symbols of the paradifferential cubic forms for the energy correction
E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)
, leaving only lower-order integrals.

This choice of energy satisfies the norm equivalence

E_{lin}(w,r)=(1+O({\mathcal{A}_{0}}))\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

We proceed to compute its time derivative:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}=2\Im\int\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}w_{\alpha t}\,d\alpha+\Im\int\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot T_{\partial_{t}J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}w_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int r\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}\bar{r}_{t}\,d\alpha+\Re\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{\frac{1}{4}}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha.

By taking the
\alpha
-derivative on the first equation of (
3.1
) and using the definition of
b_{\alpha}
, we obtain:

(3.4)

T_{D_{t}}w_{\alpha}=-T_{1-\bar{Y}}r_{\alpha\alpha}+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r_{\alpha}-2T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}-T_{T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}-T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w+G_{1},

where the error term
G_{1}
satisfies

\|G_{1}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}\|(w,r)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{3}{4}}}.

To utilize the expressions of
(T_{D_{t}}w_{\alpha},T_{D_{t}}r)
, we integrate by parts:

2\Re\int r\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}T_{b}\bar{r}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=-2\Re\int T_{\big(J^{\frac{1}{4}}b\big)_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}T_{b}r_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|r\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

so that using the fact that
J^{s}
and
b
are real-valued functions,

2\Im\int\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}T_{b}w_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha=0,\quad 2\Re\int r\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}T_{b}\bar{r}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha=-\Re\int T_{\big(J^{\frac{1}{4}}b\big)_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|r\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Using above identities, we plug in para-material derivatives in (
3.1
) and (
3.4
) to compute

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Im\int\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}T_{D_{t}}w_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+\Im\int\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot T_{\partial_{t}J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}w_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int r\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}T_{D_{t}}\bar{r}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\Re\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{\frac{1}{4}}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha+\Re\int T_{\big(J^{\frac{1}{4}}b\big)_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha+\Re\int w\cdot\bar{w}_{t}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}r_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+4\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+2\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+10\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+10\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-2\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+5\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha
