\displaystyle+2\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\bar{w}\,d\alpha-15\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\Re\int T_{\partial_{t}J^{\frac{1}{4}}+J^{\frac{1}{4}}b_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\Re\int iT_{\partial_{t}J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}G_{1}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int r\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}\bar{K}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

For the first two terms in
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
, we integrate by parts to shift
\alpha
-derivatives from
r
to
\bar{w}
and the para-coefficients,

\displaystyle 2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}r_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}r_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}r_{\alpha}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha-\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\frac{9}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+\frac{45}{8}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{45}{8}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\frac{25}{8}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{65}{8}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}\,d\alpha-5\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha-7\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{5}{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{45}{8}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\frac{65}{8}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{35}{4}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha.

For the
w\cdot\bar{w}
type integrals in
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
, we again integrate by parts and use Lemma
2.3
,

\displaystyle 4\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+2\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\Re\int iT_{\partial_{t}J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{11}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{3}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{11}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{3}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)}\bar{R}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|w\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{7}{2}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{5}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}R}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|w\|^{2}_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

Combining these results with Lemma
2.3
, we compute:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)=\frac{7}{2}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha-\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{15}{2}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{9}{2}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+5\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+3\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{5}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{3}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}R}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{3}{2}\Re\int T_{T_{(1-\bar{Y})J^{\frac{1}{4}}}R_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{55}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{45}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{35}{4}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{7}{2}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{5}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{3}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}R}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha-\frac{9}{2}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w\,d\alpha-\frac{15}{2}\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-5\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}}w_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}}w\,d\alpha+\frac{3}{2}\Re\int T_{T_{(1-\bar{Y})J^{\frac{1}{4}}}R_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha-\frac{55}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{45}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{35}{4}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

The expression for
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
contains both non-perturbative cubic and quartic integral terms, which we eliminate by constructing cubic and quartic energy corrections.
For the cubic energy corrections, we seek a cubic energy
E_{cor}^{3}(w,r)=O({\mathcal{A}_{0}})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}
such that its time derivative eliminates the cubic integral terms of
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
.
In other words, the time derivative of
E_{cor}^{3}(w,r)
needs to satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E_{cor}^{3}(w,r)=-\frac{7}{2}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha}}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-\frac{5}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}\Re\int iT_{T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}R}w\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{w}\,d\alpha+\frac{9}{2}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}}\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+3\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w\,d\alpha+\frac{15}{2}\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}w_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 5\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}}w_{\alpha}\,d\alpha+2\Re\int i\bar{r}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}}w\,d\alpha-\frac{3}{2}\Re\int T_{T_{(1-\bar{Y})J^{\frac{1}{4}}}R_{\alpha}}r\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

To achieve this, we employ a cubic energy correction of the form

\displaystyle E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)=\Re

\displaystyle\int A_{lhh}\left(R,T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}w,\bar{r}\right)+B_{lhh}\left(R,T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}r,\bar{w}\right)

\displaystyle+C_{lhh}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)}w,\bar{w}\right)+D_{lhh}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}(1-Y)}r,\bar{r}\right)\,d\alpha,

where
A_{lhh},B_{lhh},C_{lhh},D_{lhh}
are paradifferential cubic forms where the first variable is at low frequency compared to the other two variables.
The para-coefficients such as
T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}
are added to the cubic forms in order to match the para-coefficients in cubic integral terms of
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
.
We compute the time derivative of
E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)
.
It is given by

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)=\text{quartic and higher integral terms}+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\Re\int

\displaystyle-A\left(T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R,w,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}\right)+B\left(T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,\bar{w}\right)-C\left(T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha},w,\bar{w}\right)\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\Re\int

\displaystyle B\left(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}}r,\bar{w}\right)-C\left(\mathbf{W},T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}}r_{\alpha},\bar{w}\right)-D\left(\mathbf{W},T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}}r,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w}\right)\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\Re\int

\displaystyle A\left(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}}w,\bar{r}\right)-C\left(\mathbf{W},T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}}w,\bar{r}_{\alpha}\right)+D\left(\mathbf{W},iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,\bar{r}\right)\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\Re\int

\displaystyle A\left(T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R,r_{\alpha},\bar{r}\right)+B\left(T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R,r,\bar{r}_{\alpha}\right)+D\left(T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})}R_{\alpha},r,\bar{r}\right)\,d\alpha.

Let
\mathfrak{a}(\xi,\eta,\zeta)
denote the symbol of
A\left(R,T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}w,\bar{r}\right)
.
Other three symbols are defined in the same way.
We take the Fourier transform of the integral and compare symbols for each cubic term.
Using integration by parts, a derivative on the third factor is equal to the minus of the sum of derivatives of the first two factors in the integral, which shows the symbolic relation
\zeta=\xi+\eta
.
We get that symbols
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d}
solve the following algebraic linear system.

\begin{cases}\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{a}-\eta^{4}\mathfrak{b}+\xi\mathfrak{c}=\left(\frac{7}{2}\xi\eta\zeta^{2}+\frac{5}{4}\xi^{2}\zeta^{2}+\frac{3}{4}\xi^{3}\zeta\right)\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\xi^{4}\mathfrak{b}-\eta\mathfrak{c}-\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{d}=(-3\xi\zeta^{3}+\frac{9}{2}\xi^{2}\zeta^{2}-3\xi^{3}\zeta)\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\xi^{4}\mathfrak{a}+\zeta\mathfrak{c}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{d}=(3\xi\eta^{3}+\frac{15}{2}\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+5\xi^{3}\eta+2\xi^{4})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}-\zeta\mathfrak{b}+\xi\mathfrak{d}=\frac{3}{2}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\end{cases}

where the
\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
is a non-negative smooth bump function defined in (
A.18
) that selects low-high frequency portion of a product.
This algebraic system has the solution

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}=

\displaystyle\frac{5(15\xi^{6}+60\xi^{5}\eta+125\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+157\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+120\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+51\xi\eta^{5}+8\eta^{6})}{4\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5(14\xi^{6}+49\xi^{5}\eta+77\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+60\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+13\xi^{2}\eta^{4}-13\xi\eta^{5}-8\eta^{6})}{4\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{125\xi^{9}+875\xi^{8}\eta+2850\xi^{7}\eta^{2}+5633\xi^{6}\eta^{3}+7407\xi^{5}\eta^{4}}{4(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle+\frac{6708\xi^{4}\eta^{5}+4134\xi^{3}\eta^{6}+1600\xi^{2}\eta^{7}+300\xi\eta^{8}}{4(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}=

\displaystyle\frac{40\xi^{6}+105\xi^{5}\eta+135\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+83\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+25\xi^{2}\eta^{5}}{2(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

By the choice of the frequency cutoff
\chi_{1}
,
|\xi|\ll|\eta|\approx|\zeta|
.
According to our discussion in Appendix
C
, the denominators of above symbols are elliptic such that they cannot be zero.
The leading part of the numerators and denominators are powers of
\eta
.
Hence, at the leading order

\mathfrak{a}\approx\frac{2}{5}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}\approx\frac{2}{5}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}\approx 3\xi\eta^{2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}\approx\frac{1}{2}\xi^{2}\eta^{-2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

so that we have

\displaystyle E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{5}\Re\int T_{R}T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}w\cdot\bar{r}+T_{R}T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}r\cdot\bar{w}\,d\alpha-3\Re\int iT_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-Y)}w_{\alpha}\cdot\bar{w}_{\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\Re\int T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}}T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}r\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{r}\,d\alpha+\text{lower order integrals}.

As a consequence,

|E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)|\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

According to our computation of the paradifferential quadratic normal forms,
\frac{d}{dt}E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)
eliminates the cubic energy produced by
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
.
When the time derivative acts on para-coefficients such as
T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}
, it produces perturbative terms.
For instance,

\Re\int T_{R}T_{\partial_{t}J^{\frac{1}{4}}}w\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha=-\frac{1}{4}\Re\int T_{R}T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}b_{\alpha}}w\cdot\bar{r}\,d\alpha+\text{lower order integrals}=O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

The quintic integral terms produced by
\frac{d}{dt}E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)
are all perturbative.
However,
\frac{d}{dt}E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)
also produces extra non-perturbative quartic integral terms.
We get that

\frac{d}{dt}E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)+\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)=\text{non-perturbative quartic integrals}+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2},

where the non-perturbative quartic integral terms can be classified as one of following two types:

(1)

The non-perturbative quartic integral terms in
\frac{d}{dt}E_{lin}(w,r)
, namely

\displaystyle-\frac{55}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{45}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+\frac{35}{4}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha.

Note that for first two quartic integral terms of this type, the frequencies of two variables at low frequency have the same sign.

(2)

The non-perturbative quartic integral terms in
\frac{d}{dt}E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)
.
They are produced by sub-leading terms of
(w_{t},r_{t})
.
These quartic integrals are given by

3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha-3\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha.

These non-perturbative quartic integrals
I_{non}^{4}
satisfy

|I_{non}^{4}|=O(\mathcal{A}_{1}^{2})\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{4}}}^{2}
.
The linearized variables have
\frac{1}{2}
more derivative compared to
\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}
.

To eliminate these non-perturbative quartic integrals, we consider two scenarios, the non-resonant quartic integrals, and the quartic integrals that may have four-wave resonances.

(1)

For non-perturbative quartic integrals

-\frac{55}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\frac{21}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha,

two variables at low frequency are either
\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}
or
\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}
, so that their frequencies have the same sign.
According to the discussion in Appendix
C
, four-wave resonances cannot happen.
Therefore, one can construct a quartic integral correction
E^{4,1}_{cor}(w,r)=O(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2})\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{4,1}_{cor}(w,r)=\frac{55}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{21}{8}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-Y)\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+\text{quintic and higher integrals}.

These quintic and higher integrals have one or more lower order compare to these non-perturbative quartic integrals, so that these terms are perturbative.

(2)

Non-perturbative quartic integrals

\frac{47}{4}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

may have resonances.
We cannot construct quartic integral corrections to eliminate these terms without producing extra quartic integrals.
However, we can choose the correction

E^{4,2}_{cor}(w,r)=-\frac{47}{8}\Re\int\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}r\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{3}{4}}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{r}\,d\alpha.

This energy satisfies the norm equivalence
E^{4,2}_{cor}(w,r)=O(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2})\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}
, and its time derivative equals

\displaystyle E^{4,2}_{cor}(w,r)=

\displaystyle-\frac{47}{4}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle-\frac{47}{8}\Re\int\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}r\cdot T_{\partial_{t}(J^{-\frac{3}{4}}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2})}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{r}\,d\alpha+\text{ quintic and higher integrals}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{47}{4}\Re\int ir\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}.

To conclude this section, by choosing the modified energy

(3.5)

E^{0,para}_{lin}(w,r):=E_{lin}(w,r)+E^{3}_{cor}(w,r)+E^{4,1}_{cor}(w,r)+E^{4,2}_{cor}(w,r),

it satisfies the norm equivalence, and its time derivative is perturbative.
Hence, we prove Proposition
3.2
.

3.2.
Normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})

In this section, we compute normal form variables
(w_{NF}^{0},r_{NF}^{0})
such that

(3.6)

\|(w_{NF}^{0},r_{NF}^{0})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}},

and they solve the system

(3.7)

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}w_{NF}^{0}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{NF}^{0}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w_{NF}^{0}=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}+G,\\
T_{D_{t}}r_{NF}^{0}-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w_{NF}^{0}=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}+K,\end{cases}

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
2.9
).

Recall that in Lemma
2.5
, source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
are rewritten as the sum of low-high and balanced paraproducts in Lemma
2.5
.
We will construct
(w_{NF}^{0},r_{NF}^{0})
as the sum of low-high quadratic normal form variables, balanced quadratic normal form variables, and cubic normal form variables

(3.8)

(w_{NF}^{0},r_{NF}^{0}):=(w^{0}_{lh},r^{0}_{lh})+(w_{bal}^{0},r_{bal}^{0})+(w^{0}_{c},r^{0}_{c}).

Normal form variables
(w^{0}_{lh},r^{0}_{lh})
and
(w_{bal}^{0},r_{bal}^{0})
eliminate the low-high and balanced part of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
, and
(w^{0}_{c},r^{0}_{c})
eliminates the extra non-perturbative cubic part of source terms produced by quadratic normal form variables.

3.2.1.
Low-high quadratic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})

We begin by computing low-high quadratic normal form variables
(w^{0}_{lh},r^{0}_{lh})
to eliminate the low-high portion of the
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
.
That is, we seek
(w^{0}_{lh},r^{0}_{lh})
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{0}_{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{0}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}\mathbf{W}-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}R+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{0}_{lh}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{0}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{\bar{r}_{\alpha}}R-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+K.

We consider low-high normal form transformations as the sum of low-high paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{0}_{lh}

\displaystyle=B^{0}_{lh}\left(\bar{w},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\mathbf{W}\right)+C^{0}_{lh}\left(\bar{r},T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}R\right),

\displaystyle r^{0}_{lh}

\displaystyle=A^{0}_{lh}\left(\bar{r},T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}\right)+D^{0}_{lh}\left(\bar{w},T_{(1-\bar{Y})(1+\mathbf{W})}R\right).

Using these bilinear forms, we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{0}_{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{0}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{0}_{lh}(\bar{r},\mathbf{W})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{0}_{lh}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{0}_{lh}(\bar{r},i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}B^{0}_{lh}(\bar{w},R_{\alpha})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}C^{0}_{lh}(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w},R)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}\partial_{\alpha}D^{0}_{lh}(\bar{w},R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{0}_{lh}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{0}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{0}_{lh}(\bar{r},R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{0}_{lh}(\bar{r},R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{0}_{lh}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}A^{0}_{lh}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{0}_{lh}(\bar{w},\mathbf{W})+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}D^{0}_{lh}(\bar{w},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}).

We write
\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{lh}(\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A^{0}_{lh}(\bar{r},T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W})
, and similarly for other low-high bilinear forms.
To match the low-high part of paradifferential source terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
, the paradifferential symbols must satisfy the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{lh}+\eta\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{lh}+\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{lh}=-\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{lh}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{lh}-(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{lh}=-\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\zeta\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{lh}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{lh}-\eta\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{lh}=\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\eta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{lh}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{lh}-\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{lh}=\left(\frac{3}{2}\eta^{4}-\eta^{3}\zeta-\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\eta\zeta^{3}\right)\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\end{cases}

where the symbol
\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)
is defined in (
A.18
) to select the low-high frequencies.
The expressions for low-high paradifferential symbols are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{lh}=

\displaystyle\frac{5(2\eta^{6}-5\eta^{5}\zeta+12\eta^{4}\zeta-13\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+12\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-5\eta\zeta^{5}+2\zeta^{6})}{2(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{lh}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\eta^{6}-11\eta^{5}\zeta+11\eta^{4}\zeta-17\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}-25\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}+25\eta\zeta^{5}-25\zeta^{6}}{2(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{lh}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5\zeta(\eta^{2}-\eta\zeta+\zeta^{2})}{4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6}}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{lh}=

\displaystyle-\frac{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+49\eta^{4}\zeta-58\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6}}{2(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).

The leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{lh}\approx\frac{1}{5}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{lh}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{lh}\approx-\frac{1}{5}\zeta^{-3}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{lh}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).

Consequently, we obtain

\displaystyle w^{0}_{lh}=-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}+\frac{i}{5}T_{\bar{r}}T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-3}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r^{0}_{lh}=\frac{1}{5}T_{\bar{r}}T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})(1+\mathbf{W})}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}R+\text{lower order terms},

which satisfy the estimate

\|(w^{0}_{lh},r^{0}_{lh})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

3.2.2.
Balanced quadratic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})

Next, we compute balanced quadratic normal form variables
(w^{0}_{bal},r^{0}_{bal})
to eliminate the balanced portion of the
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
.
In other words, we seek
(w^{0}_{bal},r^{0}_{bal})
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{0}_{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{0}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},R)+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{0}_{bal}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{0}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{1-\bar{Y}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{w},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\bar{w}_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})+K.

We consider balanced normal form transformations as the sum of balanced paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{0}_{bal}

\displaystyle=B^{0}_{bal}\left(\bar{w},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\mathbf{W}\right)+C^{0}_{bal}\left(\bar{r},T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}R\right),

\displaystyle r^{0}_{bal}

\displaystyle=A^{0}_{bal}\left(\bar{r},T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}\right)+D^{0}_{bal}\left(\bar{w},T_{(1-\bar{Y})(1+\mathbf{W})}R\right).

For these bilinear forms, we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{0}_{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{0}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{0}_{bal}(\bar{r},\mathbf{W})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{0}_{bal}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{0}_{bal}(\bar{r},i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}B^{0}_{bal}(\bar{w},R_{\alpha})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}C^{0}_{bal}(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w},R)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}(1+\mathbf{W})}\partial_{\alpha}D^{0}_{bal}(\bar{w},R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{0}_{bal}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{0}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{0}_{bal}(\bar{r},R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{0}_{bal}(\bar{r},R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{0}_{bal}(\bar{r}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}A^{0}_{bal}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{w},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{0}_{bal}(\bar{w},\mathbf{W})+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}D^{0}_{bal}(\bar{w},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}).

We write
\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{bal}(\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A^{0}_{bal}(\bar{r},T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W})
, and similarly for other balanced bilinear forms.
To match the balanced part of the paradifferential source terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})
, the paradifferential symbols must solve the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{bal}+\eta\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{bal}+\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{bal}=-\eta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\\
\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{bal}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{bal}-(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{bal}=-\eta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\\
\zeta\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{bal}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{bal}-\eta\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{bal}=\eta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\\
\eta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{bal}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{bal}-\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{bal}=\left(\frac{3}{2}\eta^{4}-\eta^{3}\zeta-\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\eta\zeta^{3}\right)\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\end{cases}

where the symbol
\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)
is defined in (
A.19
) to select the balanced frequencies, and the indicator function
1_{\zeta<\eta}
represents the holomorphic projection.
The expressions for the balanced paradifferential symbols are:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{bal}=

\displaystyle\frac{5(2\eta^{6}-5\eta^{5}\zeta+12\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-13\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+12\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-5\eta\zeta^{5}+2\zeta^{6})}{2(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{bal}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\eta^{6}-11\eta^{5}\zeta+11\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-17\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}-25\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}+25\eta\zeta^{5}-25\zeta^{6}}{2(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{bal}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5\zeta(\eta^{2}-\eta\zeta+\zeta^{2})}{4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6}}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{bal}=

\displaystyle-\frac{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+49\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-58\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6}}{2(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}.

The leading terms of these symbols are

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{bal}\approx\frac{1}{5}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{bal}\approx-\frac{4}{5}\eta\zeta^{-1}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{bal}\approx-\frac{1}{5}\zeta^{-3}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{bal}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\eta\zeta^{-1}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}.

Hence, we obtain the estimate

\|(w^{0}_{bal},r^{0}_{bal})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{0}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

Moreover, the cubic and higher-order terms produced by the balanced normal form variables
(w^{0}{bal},r^{0}_{bal})
are perturbative.

3.2.3.
Cubic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0})

Finally, we construct cubic normal form variables
(w^{0}_{c},r^{0}_{c})
so that
(w{NF}^{0},r{NF}^{0})
satisfy (
3.7
). Recall that the quadratic normal form variables
(w^{0}_{lh}+w^{0}_{bal},r^{0}_{lh}+r^{0}_{bal})
satisfy the system

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}(w^{0}_{lh}+w^{0}_{bal})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}(r^{0}_{lh}+r^{0}_{bal})+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}(w^{0}_{lh}+w^{0}_{bal})=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{0}+\mathcal{G}_{0}^{[3]}+G,\\
T_{D_{t}}(r^{0}_{lh}+r^{0}_{bal})-i\mathcal{L}_{para}(w^{0}_{lh}+w^{0}_{bal})=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{0}+\mathcal{K}_{0}^{[3]}+K,\end{cases}

where the non-perturbative cubic terms
(\mathcal{G}0^{[3]},\mathcal{K}0^{[3]})
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{0}^{[3]}=\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{0}^{[3]}=-\frac{5}{2}iT_{\bar{w}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{1}{2}iT_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}.

To eliminate non-resonant terms in
(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{[3]},\mathcal{K}_{0}^{[3]})

\Big(-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha},-\frac{1}{2}iT_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}\Big),

we consider the system of equations for auxiliary normal form transformations
(\mathbf{W}^{0,1}_{c},R^{0,1}_{c})
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{0,1}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{0,1}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\text{cubic and higher terms},\\
\partial_{t}R^{0,1}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{0,1}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}iT_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+\text{cubic and higher terms}.\end{cases}

Auxiliary normal form transformations
(\mathbf{W}^{0,1}_{c},R^{0,1}_{c})
can be chosen as the sum of low-high paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{0,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle B^{0,1}_{c}\left(\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}\right)+C^{0,1}_{c}\left(\bar{R},T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}R\right),

\displaystyle R^{0,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle A^{0,1}_{c}\left(\bar{R},T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\mathbf{W}\right)+D^{0,1}_{c}\left(\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{(1-\bar{Y})}R\right).

A direct computation for
(\mathbf{W}^{0,1}_{c},R^{0,1}_{c})
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{0,1}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{0,1}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{0,1}_{c}(\bar{R},\mathbf{W})-T_{(1-Y)^{2}}B^{0,1}_{c}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{(1-Y)^{2}}C^{0,1}_{c}(\bar{R},i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}B^{0,1}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},R_{\alpha})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}C^{0,1}_{c}(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}},R)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{0,1}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}R^{0}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{0}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}A^{0}_{c}(\bar{R},R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{0}_{c}(\bar{R},R)-T_{1-Y}D^{0}_{c}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}A^{0}_{c}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{0}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\mathbf{W})+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}D^{0}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}).

Let
\mathfrak{a}^{0,1}_{c}(\eta,\zeta)
denote the symbol of
A^{0,1}_{c}(\bar{R},T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\mathbf{W})
, and similarly for other low-high bilinear forms. To match the low-high part of the paradifferential source terms, the paradifferential symbols must satisfy the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{0,1}_{c}+\eta\mathfrak{b}^{0,1}_{c}+\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0,1}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}\zeta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{0,1}_{c}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0,1}_{c}-(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{0,1}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\zeta\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{c}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0}_{c}-\eta\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{c}=0,\\
\eta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{0}_{c}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{0}_{c}-\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{0}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}\eta\zeta^{3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\end{cases}

where the symbol
\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)
is defined in (
A.18
) to select the low-high frequencies.
The solution to this system is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{0,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta(-2\eta^{7}+11\eta^{6}\zeta-33\eta^{5}\zeta^{2}+58\eta^{4}\zeta^{3}-71\eta^{3}\zeta^{4}+52\eta^{2}\zeta^{5}-25\eta\zeta^{6}+5\zeta^{7})}{2\eta(\zeta-\eta)(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-54\eta^{3}\eta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{0,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{\eta(\eta^{2}-\eta\zeta+\zeta^{2})(2\eta^{3}\zeta-\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}+2\eta\zeta^{3}+5\zeta^{4})}{2(\zeta-\eta)(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-54\eta^{3}\eta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{0,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{-2\eta^{5}+3\eta^{4}\zeta-7\eta^{3}\zeta^{2}-2\eta^{2}\zeta^{3}+5\eta\zeta^{4}+5\zeta^{5}}{2\eta(\zeta-\eta)(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-54\eta^{3}\eta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{0,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{-2\eta^{7}+11\eta^{6}\zeta-33\eta^{5}\zeta^{2}+63\eta^{4}\zeta^{3}-76\eta^{3}\zeta^{4}+52\eta^{2}\zeta^{5}-20\eta\zeta^{6}}{2(\zeta-\eta)(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-54\eta^{3}\eta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle w^{0,1}_{c}=\frac{1}{10}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+\frac{i}{10}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\bar{R}}T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-2}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r^{0,1}_{c}=-\frac{1}{10}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\frac{2}{5}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}R+\text{lower order terms}.

We set
(w^{0,1}_{c},r^{0,1}_{c})=\big(T_{\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}^{0}_{c},T_{\bar{w}}R^{0}_{c}\big)
. Then

\|(w^{0,1}_{c},r^{0,1}_{c})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}},

and this pair satisfies the system

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}w^{0,1}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{0,1}_{c}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w^{0,1}_{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{10}\bar{w}_{t}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{10}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\bar{R}}T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-2}R+G+\text{quartic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}r^{0,1}_{c}-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w^{0,1}_{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}iT_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{1}{10}T_{\bar{w}_{t}}T_{\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{5}T_{\bar{w}_{t}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial^{-1}_{\alpha}R+K+\text{quartic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}iT_{\bar{w}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\bar{R}}T_{1-Y}R_{\alpha}+K.

The remaining terms in
(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{[3]},\mathcal{K}_{0}^{[3]})
may have resonances.
To eliminate these terms, as above, we consider the system of equations for auxiliary normal form transformations
(\mathbf{W}^{0,2}_{c},R^{0,2}_{c})
,

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{0,2}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{0,2}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\text{cubic and higher terms},\\
\partial_{t}R^{0,2}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{0,2}_{c}=\frac{5}{2}iT_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+\text{cubic and higher terms}.\end{cases}

We consider auxiliary normal form transformations as the sum of low-high paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\mathbf{W}^{0,2}_{c}=B^{0,2}_{c}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}\right)+C^{0,2}_{c}\left(R,T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}R\right),\quad R^{0,2}_{c}=A^{0,2}_{c}\left(R,T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}\right)+D^{0,2}_{c}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{1-Y}R\right).

Then, we find
(\mathbf{W}^{0,2}_{c},R^{0,2}_{c})
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{0,2}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{0,2}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{0,2}_{c}(R,\mathbf{W})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{0,2}_{c}(R_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{0,2}_{c}(R,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{J^{-1}}B^{0,2}_{c}(\mathbf{W},R_{\alpha})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{0,2}_{c}(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},R)+T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{0,2}_{c}(\mathbf{W},R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}R^{0,2}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{0,2}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{0,2}_{c}(R,R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{0,2}_{c}(R,R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{0,2}_{c}(R_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{0,2}_{c}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{0,2}_{c}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{0,2}_{c}(\mathbf{W},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}).

Here, we write
\mathfrak{a}^{0,2}_{c}(\xi,\eta)
for the symbol of
A^{0,2}_{c}(R,T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W})
, and similarly for other low-high bilinear forms.
To match the low-high part of paradifferential source terms, paradifferential symbols solve the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{0,2}_{c}-\xi\mathfrak{b}^{0,2}_{c}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0,2}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{0,2}_{c}-\xi^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0,2}_{c}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{0,2}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{0,2}_{c}+(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{0,2}_{c}+\xi\mathfrak{d}^{0,2}_{c}=0,\\
\xi^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{0,2}_{c}-(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{0,2}_{c}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{0,2}_{c}=\frac{5}{2}\xi\eta^{3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\end{cases}

where the symbol
\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)
is defined in (
A.18
) to select the low-high frequencies.
The solution of above system is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{0,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{-5\xi^{7}-30\xi^{6}\eta-107\xi^{5}\eta^{2}-183\xi^{4}\eta^{3}-187\xi^{3}\eta^{4}-133\xi^{2}\eta^{5}-42\xi\eta^{6}-5\eta^{7}}{2\xi(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{0,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{-5\xi^{6}-10\xi^{5}\eta-37\xi^{4}\eta^{2}-85\xi^{3}\eta^{3}-112\xi^{2}\eta^{4}-85\xi\eta^{5}-30\eta^{6}}{2(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{0,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{5\xi^{5}+12\xi^{4}\eta+15\xi^{3}\eta^{2}+25\xi^{2}\eta^{3}+22\xi\eta^{4}+5\eta^{5}}{2\xi\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{0,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5\xi^{7}+32\xi^{6}\eta+88\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+147\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+158\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+132\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+80\xi\eta^{6}+30\eta^{7}}{2\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle w^{0,2}_{c}=-\frac{3}{5}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}-\frac{i}{10}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-2}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r^{0,2}_{c}=-\frac{1}{10}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{3}{5}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{1-Y}R+\text{lower order terms}.

Choose
(w^{0,2}_{c},r^{0,2}_{c})=\big(T_{\bar{w}}\mathbf{W}^{0}_{c},T_{\bar{w}}T_{1-Y}R^{0}_{c}\big)
. It then follows that

\|(w^{0,2}_{c},r^{0,2}_{c})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

This pair satisfies

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}w^{0,2}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{0,2}_{c}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w^{0,2}_{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}-\frac{3}{5}T_{\bar{w}_{t}}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{10}T_{\bar{w}_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-2}R+\text{quartic and higher terms}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{w}}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}r^{0,2}_{c}-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w^{0,2}_{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}iT_{\bar{w}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{1}{10}T_{\bar{w}_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{3}{5}T_{\bar{w}_{t}}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}R

\displaystyle+\text{quartic and higher terms}+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{5}{2}iT_{\bar{w}}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+K.

As a consequence of above computations, the cubic normal form variables
(w^{0}_{c},r^{0}_{c}):=(w^{0,1}_{c},r^{0,1}_{c})+(w^{0,2}_{c},r^{0,2}_{c})
solve the system

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}w^{0}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{0}_{c}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w^{0}_{c}=-\mathcal{G}_{0}^{[3]}+G,\\
T_{D_{t}}r^{0}_{c}-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w^{0}_{c}=-\mathcal{K}_{0}^{[3]}+K.\end{cases}

To conclude this subsection, by choosing normal form variables
(w_{NF}^{0},r_{NF}^{0})
as in (
3.8
), they satisfy (
3.6
) and solve the system (
3.7
).

3.3.
Normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})

In this section, we construct normal form variables
(w_{NF}^{1},r_{NF}^{1})
satisfying

(3.9)

\|(w_{NF}^{1},r_{NF}^{1})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}},

such that they solve the system

(3.10)

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}w_{NF}^{1}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r_{NF}^{1}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w_{NF}^{1}=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}+G,\\
T_{D_{t}}r_{NF}^{1}-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w_{NF}^{1}=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}+K,\end{cases}

where
(G,K)
are perturbative source terms that satisfy (
2.9
).

Recall that source terms
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
are rewritten as the sum of low-high and balanced paraproducts in Lemma
2.6
.
We will construct
(w_{NF}^{1},r_{NF}^{1})
as the sum of low-high quadratic normal form variables, balanced quadratic normal form variables, and cubic normal form variables

(3.11)

(w_{NF}^{1},r_{NF}^{1}):=(w^{1}_{lh},r^{1}_{lh})+(w_{bal}^{1},r_{bal}^{1})+(w^{1}_{c},r^{1}_{c}).

Normal form variables
(w^{1}_{lh},r^{1}_{lh})
and
(w_{bal}^{1},r_{bal}^{1})
eliminate the low-high and balanced part of
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
, and
(w^{1}_{c},r^{1}_{c})
eliminates the extra non-perturbative cubic part of source terms produced by quadratic normal form variables.

3.3.1.
Low-high quadratic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})

We first construct the low‑high quadratic normal form variables
(w^{1}_{lh},r^{1}_{lh})
to eliminate the low-high portion of the
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
.
That is, we seek
(w^{1}_{lh},r^{1}_{lh})
satisfying

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{1}_{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{1}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{w}R_{\alpha}-T_{w_{\alpha}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{1}_{lh}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{1}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}T_{r_{\alpha}}R-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{w_{\alpha\alpha}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha}

\displaystyle-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{3}w}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w}\mathbf{W}+K.

We consider low-high normal form transformations as the sum of low-high paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{1}_{lh}

\displaystyle=B^{1}_{lh}\left(w,T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}\right)+C^{1}_{lh}\left(r,T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}R\right),

\displaystyle r^{1}_{lh}

\displaystyle=A^{1}_{lh}\left(r,T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}\right)+D^{1}_{lh}\left(w,R\right).

We then compute using the above bilinear forms,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{1}_{lh}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{1}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{1}_{lh}(r,\mathbf{W})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{1}_{lh}(r_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{1}_{lh}(r,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}B^{1}_{lh}(w,R_{\alpha})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{1}_{lh}(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,R)+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{1}_{lh}(w,R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{1}_{lh}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{1}_{lh}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{1}_{lh}(r,R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{1}_{lh}(r,R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{1}_{lh}(r_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{1}_{lh}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{1}_{lh}(w,\mathbf{W})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{1}_{lh}(w,\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}).

We write
\mathfrak{a}^{1}_{lh}(\xi,\eta)
for the symbol of
A^{1}_{lh}(r,T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W})
, and similarly for other low-high bilinear forms.
To match the low-high part of paradifferential source terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
, paradifferential symbols solve the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{1}_{lh}-\xi\mathfrak{b}^{1}_{lh}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1}_{lh}=0,\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{1}_{lh}-\xi^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1}_{lh}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{1}_{lh}=(\xi+\eta)\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{1}_{lh}+(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1}_{lh}+\xi\mathfrak{d}^{1}_{lh}=\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\xi^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{1}_{lh}-(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{1}_{lh}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{1}_{lh}=-\left(\eta^{4}+\frac{5}{2}\xi\eta^{3}+5\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+5\xi^{3}\eta+\xi^{4}\right)\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\end{cases}

where the symbol
\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)
is defined in (
A.18
) to select the low-high frequencies. The expressions for the low-high paradifferential symbols are provided below.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{1}_{lh}=

\displaystyle\frac{10\xi^{7}+70\xi^{6}\eta+170\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+227\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+184\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+80\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+5\xi\eta^{6}+10\eta^{7}}{2\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{1}_{lh}=

\displaystyle\frac{10\xi^{7}+80\xi^{6}\eta+240\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+397\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+427\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+300\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+125\xi\eta^{6}+25\eta^{7}}{2\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{1}_{lh}=

\displaystyle\frac{8\xi^{4}+18\xi^{3}\eta+20\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+15\xi\eta^{3}+5\eta^{4}}{\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{1}_{lh}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(8\xi^{3}+10\xi^{2}\eta+10\xi\eta^{2}+5\eta^{3})(2\xi^{4}+5\xi^{3}\eta+10\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+10\xi\eta^{3}+5\eta^{4})}{2\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

The leading terms of these symbols are

\mathfrak{a}^{1}_{lh}\approx\frac{1}{5}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{1}_{lh}\approx\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{1}_{lh}\approx\frac{1}{5}\eta^{-3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{1}_{lh}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle w^{1}_{lh}=\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}-\frac{i}{5}T_{r}T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-3}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r^{1}_{lh}=\frac{1}{5}T_{r}T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}R+\text{lower order terms},

so that they satisfy the estimate

\|(w^{1}_{lh},r^{1}_{lh})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

3.3.2.
Balanced quadratic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})

Next, we compute balanced quadratic normal form variables
(w^{1}_{bal},r^{1}_{bal})
to eliminate the balanced portion of the
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
. That is, we seek
(w^{1}_{bal},r^{1}_{bal})
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{1}_{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{1}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}\Pi(w_{\alpha},R)-T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\bar{R})+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{\mathbf{W}})+G,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{1}_{bal}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{1}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},R)-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w,\mathbf{W})-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\mathbf{W}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle-5iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha})-\frac{5}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})-iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\Pi(w,\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(r_{\alpha},\bar{R})-\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{4}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(\partial^{3}_{\alpha}w,\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha\alpha},\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha})+\frac{3}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}{\mathbf{P}}\Pi(w_{\alpha},\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}})+K.

We consider balanced normal form transformations as the sum of balanced paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{1}_{bal}

\displaystyle=B^{1,h}_{bal}\left(T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W},w\right)+C^{1,h}_{bal}\left(T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}R,r\right)+B^{1,a}_{bal}\left(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\mathbf{W}},w\right)+C^{1,a}_{bal}\left(T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\bar{R},r\right),

\displaystyle r^{1}_{bal}

\displaystyle=A^{1,h}_{bal}\left(T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W},r\right)+D^{1,h}_{bal}\left(R,w\right)+A^{1,a}_{bal}\left(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\mathbf{W}},r\right)+D^{1,a}_{bal}\left(T_{(1-Y)(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\bar{R},w\right).

For above bilinear forms, we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{1}_{bal}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{1}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{1,h}_{bal}(\mathbf{W},r)-T_{J^{-1}}B^{1,h}_{bal}(\mathbf{W},r_{\alpha})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{1,h}_{bal}(i\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W},r)

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}B^{1,h}_{bal}(R_{\alpha},w)+T_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{1,h}_{bal}(R,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w)+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{1,h}_{bal}(R,w)

\displaystyle+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},r)-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}B^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},r_{\alpha})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}C^{1,a}_{bal}(i\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}},r)

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}B^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},w)+T_{1-Y}C^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{R},i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w)+T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}D^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{R},w),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{1}_{bal}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{1}_{bal}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{1,h}_{bal}(R_{\alpha},r)-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{1,h}_{bal}(R,r)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{1,h}_{bal}(R,r_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{1,h}_{bal}(\mathbf{W},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w)-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{1,h}_{bal}(\mathbf{W},w)+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{1,h}_{bal}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},w)

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}A^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},r)-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{R},r)-T_{1-Y}D^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{R},r_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}A^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w)-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},w)-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}D^{1,h}_{bal}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}},w).

Here, we write
\mathfrak{a}^{1,h}_{bal}(\xi,\eta)
for the symbol of
A^{1,h}_{bal}(T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W},r)
,
\mathfrak{a}^{1,a}_{bal}(\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A^{1,a}_{bal}(T_{1-\bar{Y}}\bar{\mathbf{W}},r)
and similarly for other balanced bilinear forms.
To match the balanced part of paradifferential source terms in
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})
, paradifferential symbols of the holomorphic type solve the following algebraic systems

\begin{cases}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{1,h}_{bal}-\eta\mathfrak{b}^{1,h}_{bal}+\xi^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,h}_{bal}=0,\\
\xi\mathfrak{b}^{1,h}_{bal}-\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,h}_{bal}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{1,h}_{bal}=(\xi+\eta)\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\\
\xi\mathfrak{a}^{1,h}_{bal}+(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,h}_{bal}+\eta\mathfrak{d}^{1,h}_{bal}=\eta\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{1,h}_{bal}-(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{1,h}_{bal}+\xi^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{1,h}_{bal}=-\left(\frac{5}{2}\eta^{4}+5\eta^{3}\xi+5\eta^{2}\xi^{2}+\frac{5}{2}\eta\xi^{3}+\xi^{4}\right)\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta).\end{cases}

And for paradifferential symbols of the mixed type,

\begin{cases}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{1,a}_{bal}-\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{1,a}_{bal}-\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,a}_{bal}=\zeta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{1,a}_{bal}+\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,a}_{bal}+(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{1,a}_{bal}=-\zeta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{1,a}_{bal}-(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,a}_{bal}-\zeta\mathfrak{d}^{1,a}_{bal}=-\zeta\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\\
\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{1,a}_{bal}-(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{1,a}_{bal}-\eta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{1,a}_{bal}=\left(-\frac{3}{2}\zeta^{4}+\zeta^{3}\eta+\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}-\frac{3}{2}\zeta\eta^{3}\right)\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}.\end{cases}

The expressions for the balanced symbols of holomorphic type are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{1,h}_{bal}=

\displaystyle\frac{25\eta^{7}+100\eta^{6}\xi+200\eta^{5}\xi^{2}+242\eta^{4}\xi^{3}+190\eta^{3}\xi^{4}+80\eta^{2}\xi^{5}+5\xi^{6}+10\xi^{7}}{2\xi(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{1,h}_{bal}=

\displaystyle\frac{25\eta^{7}+125\eta^{6}\xi+300\eta^{5}\xi^{2}+442\eta^{4}\xi^{3}+442\eta^{3}\xi^{4}+300\eta^{2}\xi^{5}+125\eta\xi^{6}+25\xi^{7}}{2\xi(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{1,h}_{bal}=

\displaystyle\frac{5(\eta^{4}+3\eta^{3}\xi+4\eta^{2}\xi^{2}+3\xi^{3}\eta+\xi^{4})}{\xi(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{1,h}_{bal}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5(\eta^{3}+2\eta^{2}\xi+2\eta\xi^{2}+\xi^{3})(2\eta^{4}+5\eta^{3}\xi+10\eta^{2}\xi^{2}+10\eta\xi^{3}+5\xi^{4})}{2\xi(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta).

The leading terms of these balanced symbols of holomorphic type are

\mathfrak{a}^{1,h}_{bal}\approx\frac{213}{448}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{1,h}_{bal}\approx\frac{223}{224}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{1,h}_{bal}\approx\frac{15}{224}\eta^{-3}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{1,h}_{bal}\approx-\frac{15}{28}\chi_{2}(\xi,\eta).

The expressions for the balanced symbols of mixed type are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{1,a}_{bal}=

\displaystyle-\frac{8\eta^{6}\zeta-24\eta^{5}\zeta^{2}+49\eta^{4}\zeta^{3}-58\eta^{3}\zeta^{4}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{5}-50\eta\zeta^{6}+25\zeta^{7}}{2\eta(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{1,a}_{bal}=

\displaystyle-\frac{-2\eta^{6}\zeta+11\eta^{5}\zeta^{2}-11\eta^{4}\zeta^{3}+17\eta^{3}\zeta^{4}+25\eta^{2}\zeta^{5}-25\eta\zeta^{6}+25\zeta^{7}}{2\eta(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{1,a}_{bal}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5(\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}-\eta\zeta^{3}+\zeta^{4})}{\eta(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{1,a}_{bal}=

\displaystyle\frac{5(\eta^{2}\zeta-\eta\zeta^{2}+\zeta^{3})(2\eta^{4}-3\eta^{3}\zeta+7\zeta^{2}\zeta^{2}-3\eta\zeta^{3}+2\zeta^{4})}{2\eta(4\eta^{6}-12\eta^{5}\zeta+37\eta^{4}\zeta-54\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6})}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}.

The leading terms of these balanced symbols of mixed type are

\mathfrak{a}^{1,a}_{bal}\approx-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{b}^{1,a}_{bal}\approx-\frac{4}{5}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{c}^{1,a}_{bal}\approx-\frac{\eta^{-3}}{5}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta},\quad\mathfrak{d}^{1,a}_{bal}\approx\frac{1}{2}\chi_{2}(\eta,\zeta)1_{\zeta<\eta}.

Hence, we obtain that balanced normal form corrections
(w^{1}_{bal},r^{1}_{bal})
satisfy the estimate

\|(w^{1}_{bal},r^{1}_{bal})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{0}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

Moreover, the cubic and higher terms produced by balanced normal form variables
(w^{1}_{bal},r^{1}_{bal})
are perturbative.

3.3.3.
Cubic normal form analysis for
({\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1},{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1})

Finally, we construct cubic normal form variables
(w^{1}_{c},r^{1}_{c})
so that
(w_{NF}^{1},r_{NF}^{1})
solve (
3.10
).
Recall that quadratic normal form variables
(w^{1}_{lh}+w^{1}_{bal},r^{1}_{lh}+r^{1}_{bal})
solve the system

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}(w^{1}_{lh}+w^{1}_{bal})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}(r^{1}_{lh}+r^{1}_{bal})+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}(w^{1}_{lh}+w^{1}_{bal})=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{1}^{[3]}+G,\\
T_{D_{t}}(r^{1}_{lh}+r^{1}_{bal})-i\mathcal{L}_{para}(w^{1}_{lh}+w^{1}_{bal})=-{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{K}_{1}+\mathcal{K}_{1}^{[3]}+K,\end{cases}

where non-perturbative cubic terms
(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{[3]},\mathcal{K}_{1}^{[3]})
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{1}^{[3]}=-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{1}^{[3]}=\frac{25}{4}iT_{w}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+3iT_{w}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{R}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\bar{R}}T_{1-Y}R_{\alpha}.

The terms

\left(-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha},\,\frac{25}{4}iT_{w}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{R}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}\right)

are non-resonant. To eliminate these terms, we consider the system of of equations for auxiliary normal form transformations
(\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c},R^{1,1}_{c})
,

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{1,1}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\text{cubic and higher terms},\\
\partial_{t}R^{1,1}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c}=-\frac{25}{4}iT_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+\frac{1}{2}T_{R}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+\text{cubic and higher terms}.\end{cases}

Auxiliary normal form transformations
(\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c},R^{1,1}_{c})
are chosen as the sum of low-high paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle B^{1,1}_{c}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}\right)+C^{1,1}_{c}\left(R,T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}R\right),

\displaystyle R^{1,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle A^{1,1}_{c}\left(R,T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W}\right)+D^{1,1}_{c}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{1-Y}R\right).

One can verify that

(\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c},R^{1,1}_{c})
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{1,1}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{1,1}_{c}(R,\mathbf{W})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{1,1}_{c}(R_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{1,1}_{c}(R,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{J^{-1}}B^{1,1}_{c}(\mathbf{W},R_{\alpha})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{1,1}_{c}(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},R)+T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{1,1}_{c}(\mathbf{W},R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}R^{1,1}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{1,1}_{c}(R,R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{1,1}_{c}(R,R)-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{1,1}_{c}(R_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{1,1}_{c}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{1,1}_{c}(\mathbf{W},\mathbf{W})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{1,1}_{c}(\mathbf{W},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}).

Here, we write
\mathfrak{a}^{1,1}_{c}(\xi,\eta)
for the symbol of
A^{1,1}_{c}(R,T_{1-Y}\mathbf{W})
, and similarly for other low-high bilinear forms.
To match the low-high part of paradifferential source terms, paradifferential symbols solve the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{1,1}_{c}-\xi\mathfrak{b}^{1,1}_{c}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,1}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{1,1}_{c}-\xi^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,1}_{c}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{1,1}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{1,1}_{c}+(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,1}_{c}+\xi\mathfrak{d}^{1,1}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\xi^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{1,1}_{c}-(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{1,1}_{c}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{1,1}_{c}=-\frac{25}{4}\xi\eta^{3}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\end{cases}

where the symbol
\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)
is defined in (
A.18
) to select the low-high frequencies.
The solution of above system is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{1,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{10\xi^{7}+60\xi^{6}\eta+289\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+520\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+534\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+321\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+134\xi\eta^{6}+20\eta^{7}}{4\xi(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{1,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{10\xi^{6}+20\xi^{5}\eta+149\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+399\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+528\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+395\xi\eta^{5}+135\eta^{6}}{4(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{1,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5\xi^{5}+17\xi^{4}\eta+30\xi^{3}\eta^{2}+60\xi^{2}\eta^{3}+52\xi\eta^{4}+10\eta^{5}}{2\xi\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{1,1}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{10\xi^{7}+74\xi^{6}\eta+196\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+344\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+401\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+418\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+310\xi\eta^{6}+135\eta^{7}}{4\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle w^{1,1}_{c}=\frac{27}{20}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}+\frac{i}{5}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-2}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r^{1,1}_{c}=\frac{1}{5}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+\frac{27}{20}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{1-Y}R+\text{lower order terms}.

We set
(w^{1,1}_{c},r^{1,1}_{c})=\big(T_{w}\mathbf{W}^{1,1}_{c},T_{w}T_{1-Y}R^{1,1}_{c}\big)
. Then

\|(w^{1,1}_{c},r^{1,1}_{c})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}},

and this pair solves the system

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}w^{1,1}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{1,1}_{c}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w^{1,1}_{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\frac{27}{20}T_{w_{t}}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{5}T_{w_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-2}R+\text{quartic and higher terms}+G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-1}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{R}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+G,

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}r^{0,2}_{c}-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w^{0,2}_{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{25}{4}iT_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+\frac{1}{2}T_{R}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{5}T_{w_{t}}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}R}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+\frac{27}{20}T_{w_{t}}T_{\mathbf{W}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}R

\displaystyle+\text{quartic and higher terms}+K

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{25}{4}iT_{\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+\frac{1}{2}T_{R}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}+K.

To eliminate other terms in
(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{[3]},\mathcal{K}_{1}^{[3]})
that may have resonances, we consider the system of of equations for auxiliary normal form transformations
(\mathbf{W}^{1,2}_{c},R^{1,2}_{c})
,

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{1,2}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{1,2}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\text{cubic and higher terms},\\
\partial_{t}R^{1,2}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{1,2}_{c}=-3iT_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W}+\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{R}}T_{1-Y}R_{\alpha}+\text{cubic and higher terms}.\end{cases}

As above, the auxiliary normal form transformations
(\mathbf{W}^{1}_{c},R^{1}_{c})
are chosen as the sum of low-high paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\mathbf{W}^{1,2}_{c}=B^{1,2}_{c}\left(\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}\right)+C^{1,2}_{c}\left(\bar{R},T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}R\right),\quad R^{1,2}_{c}=A^{1,2}_{c}\left(\bar{R},T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\mathbf{W}\right)+D^{1,2}_{c}\left(\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}R\right).

For there bilinear forms, we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}^{1,2}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}R^{1,2}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{R},\mathbf{W})-T_{(1-Y)^{2}}B^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},\mathbf{W})+T_{(1-Y)^{2}}C^{1}_{c}(\bar{R},i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W})

\displaystyle-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}B^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},R_{\alpha})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}C^{1,2}_{c}(i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}},R)+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},R),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}R^{1,2}_{c}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}^{1}_{c}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}A^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{R},R_{\alpha})-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{R},R)-T_{1-Y}D^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},R)

\displaystyle-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}A^{1,2}_{c}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}},\mathbf{W})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\mathbf{W})+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)}D^{1,2}_{c}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W}).

Here, as above, we write
\mathfrak{a}^{1,2}_{c}(\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A^{1,2}_{c}\left(\bar{R},T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\mathbf{W}\right)
and similarly for other low-high bilinear forms.
To match the low-high part of paradifferential source terms, paradifferential symbols solve the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{1,2}_{c}+\eta\mathfrak{b}^{1}_{c}+\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,2}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}\zeta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{1,2}_{c}+\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,2}_{c}-(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{1,2}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\zeta\mathfrak{a}^{1,2}_{c}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{1,2}_{c}-\eta\mathfrak{d}^{1,2}_{c}=-\frac{1}{2}\zeta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\eta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{1,2}_{c}+(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{1,2}_{c}-\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{1,2}_{c}=-3\eta\zeta^{3}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).\end{cases}

The solutions of the above system are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{1,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{-2\eta^{7}\zeta+11\eta^{6}\zeta^{2}-43\eta^{5}\zeta^{3}+73\eta^{4}\zeta^{4}-106\eta^{3}\zeta^{5}+72\eta^{2}\zeta^{6}-40\eta\zeta^{7}+10\zeta^{8}}{\eta(\eta-\zeta)(8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{1,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\eta^{5}\zeta-\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-6\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+21\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-30\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6}}{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6}}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{1,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{-2\eta^{4}-\eta^{3}\zeta-3\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}-15\eta\zeta^{3}+10\zeta^{4}}{\eta(8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{1,2}_{c}=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\eta^{7}-13\eta^{6}\zeta+36\eta^{5}\zeta^{2}-70\eta^{4}\zeta^{3}+79\eta^{3}\zeta^{4}-29\eta^{2}\zeta^{5}-5\eta\zeta^{6}+25\zeta^{7}}{(\eta-\zeta)(8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6})}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle w^{1,2}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}+\frac{i}{5}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{R}}T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-2}R+\text{lower order terms},

\displaystyle r^{1,2}_{c}=-\frac{1}{5}T_{\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}+\frac{1}{2}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}T_{1-\bar{Y}}R+\text{lower order terms}.

Setting cubic normal form variables
(w^{1,2}_{c},r^{1,2}_{c})=(T_{w}w^{1}_{c},T_{w}r^{1}_{c})
, we obtain

\|(w^{1,2}_{c},r^{1,2}_{c})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{0}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{3}{2}}\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}.

This pair solves the system

\displaystyle T_{D_{t}}w^{1,2}_{c}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{1,2}_{c}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w^{1,2}_{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}R+\frac{1}{2}T_{w}T_{\bar{R}}T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}T_{w_{t}}T_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}T_{J^{-1}}\mathbf{W}
