\displaystyle-L(\bar{R}_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\tilde{w}^{s})+L(\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\tilde{r}^{s})-\frac{s}{2}T_{T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}^{s}+\frac{s}{2}T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\tilde{r}^{s}+G_{s},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{s}_{1}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{s}_{1}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-L(R_{\alpha},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{r}^{s})-\frac{s}{2}T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}\tilde{r}^{s}-2siT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\tilde{w}^{s}-\frac{5}{2}iL\Big(\mathbf{W}_{\alpha},T_{(1-Y)J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\tilde{w}^{s}\Big)

\displaystyle-3siT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{\alpha\alpha}-5iL\Big(\mathbf{W}_{\alpha\alpha},T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\tilde{w}^{s}\Big)-2siT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle-5iL\Big(\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\mathbf{W},T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{\alpha}\Big)-\frac{s}{2}iT_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W}}\tilde{w}^{s}-\frac{5}{2}iL\Big(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\tilde{w}^{s}\Big)

\displaystyle-iL\Big(\partial_{\alpha}^{5}\mathbf{W},T_{(1-Y)^{2}J^{-\frac{3}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{w}^{s}\Big)-L(\bar{R}_{\alpha},T_{1-Y}\tilde{r}^{s})-\frac{s}{2}T_{T_{1-Y}\bar{R}_{\alpha}}\tilde{r}^{s}-2siT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\tilde{w}^{s}

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}iL\Big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha},T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\tilde{w}^{s}\Big)-3siT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{\alpha\alpha}-iL\Big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha\alpha},T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\tilde{w}^{s}\Big)

\displaystyle-2siT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{\alpha}+iL\Big(\partial_{\alpha}^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\tilde{w}^{s}_{\alpha}\Big)+\frac{s}{2}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\tilde{w}^{s}-\frac{3}{2}iL\Big(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\tilde{w}^{s}\Big)+K_{s}.

We consider normal form transformations
(w^{s}_{1},r^{s}_{1})
as the sum of balanced paradifferential bilinear forms of the following type:

\displaystyle w^{s}_{1}

\displaystyle=B^{s}_{1,h}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{1-Y}\tilde{w}^{s}\right)+C^{s}_{1,h}\left(R,T_{J^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\mathbf{W})^{2}}\tilde{r}^{s}\right)+B^{s}_{1,a}\left(\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{w}^{s}\right)+C^{s}_{1,a}\left(\bar{R},T_{J^{\frac{3}{2}}}\tilde{r}^{s}\right),

\displaystyle r^{s}_{1}

\displaystyle=A^{s}_{1,h}\left(\mathbf{W},T_{1-Y}\tilde{r}^{s}\right)+D^{s}_{1,h}\left(R,\tilde{w}^{s}\right)+A^{s}_{1,a}\left(\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{r}^{s}\right)+D^{s}_{1,a}\left(\bar{R},T_{(1-Y)(1+\bar{\mathbf{W}})}\tilde{w}^{s}\right).

For above paradifferential bilinear forms, we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w^{s}_{1}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{s}_{1}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{J^{-1}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{s}_{1,h}(\mathbf{W},\tilde{r}^{s})-T_{J^{-1}}B^{s}_{1,h}(\mathbf{W},\tilde{r}^{s}_{\alpha})+T_{J^{-1}}C^{s}_{1,h}(i\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\mathbf{W},\tilde{r}^{s})

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}B^{s}_{1,h}(R_{\alpha},\tilde{w}^{s})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}C^{s}_{1,h}(R,i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\tilde{w}^{s})+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}D^{s}_{1,h}(R,\tilde{w}^{s})

\displaystyle+T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}A^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\tilde{r}^{s})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}B^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\tilde{r}^{s}_{\alpha})-T_{(1-\bar{Y})^{2}}C^{s}_{1,a}(i\partial^{4}_{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{W}},\tilde{r}^{s})

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}B^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},\tilde{w}^{s})+T_{1-Y}C^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{R},i\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\tilde{w}^{s})+T_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}D^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{R},\tilde{w}^{s}),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}r^{s}_{1}-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}w^{s}_{1}+\text{cubic and higher terms}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1-\bar{Y}}A^{s}_{1,h}(R_{\alpha},\tilde{r}^{s})-iT_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{s}_{1,h}(R,\tilde{r}^{s})-T_{1-\bar{Y}}D^{s}_{1,h}(R,\tilde{r}^{s}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}A^{s}_{1,h}(\mathbf{W},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\tilde{w}^{s})-iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{s}_{1,h}(\mathbf{W},\tilde{w}^{s})+iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{2}}D^{s}_{1,h}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\mathbf{W},\tilde{w}^{s})

\displaystyle-T_{1-Y}A^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{R}_{\alpha},\tilde{r}^{s})-iT_{1-Y}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}C^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{R},\tilde{r}^{s})-T_{1-Y}D^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{R},\tilde{r}^{s}_{\alpha})

\displaystyle+iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}A^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\tilde{w}^{s})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}\partial_{\alpha}^{4}B^{s}_{1,a}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},\tilde{w}^{s})-iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}}D^{s}_{1,a}(\partial_{\alpha}^{4}\bar{\mathbf{W}},\tilde{w}^{s}).

We write
\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,h}(\xi,\eta)
for the symbol of
A^{1,h}_{bal}(\mathbf{W},T_{1-Y}\tilde{r}^{s})
,
\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,a}(\eta,\zeta)
for the symbol of
A^{1,a}_{bal}(\bar{\mathbf{W}},T_{1-\bar{Y}}\tilde{r}^{s})
and similarly for other bilinear forms.
To match paradifferential source terms of holomorphic type in
(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{s},\mathcal{K}_{1}^{s})
, paradifferential symbols of the holomorphic type solve the following algebraic systems:

\begin{cases}(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,h}-\eta\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,h}+\xi^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,h}=\frac{s}{2}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\xi\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,h}-\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,h}-(\xi+\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,h}=(\xi+\xi^{2}\eta^{-1})\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)+\frac{s}{2}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\xi\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,h}+(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,h}+\eta\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,h}=\xi\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)+\frac{s}{2}\xi\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\\
\eta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,h}-(\xi+\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,h}+\xi^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,h}=-\left(\frac{5}{2}\xi\eta^{3}+5\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+5\xi^{3}\eta+\frac{5}{2}\xi^{4}+\xi^{5}\eta^{-1}\right)\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)\\
-s(2\xi\eta^{3}+3\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+2\xi^{3}\eta+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{4})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\end{cases}

where
\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)
represents the symbolic relation for the bilinear form
L
defined in (
4.11
) so that
|\xi|\ll|\eta|
.
The expressions for the bilinear symbols of holomorphic type are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,h}=

\displaystyle\frac{-10\xi^{7}+5\xi^{6}\eta+80\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+190\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+242\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+100\xi\eta^{6}+25\eta^{7}}{2\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle+\frac{-2\xi^{7}+45\xi^{6}\eta+190\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+390\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+487\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+400\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+200\xi\eta^{6}+50\eta^{7}}{4\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}s\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,h}=

\displaystyle\frac{25\xi^{7}+125\xi^{6}\eta+300\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+442\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+442\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+300\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+125\xi\eta^{6}+25\eta^{7}}{2\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle+\frac{-5\xi^{7}+35\xi^{6}\eta+180\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+385\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+487\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+400\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+200\xi\eta^{6}+50\eta^{7}}{4\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}s\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,h}=

\displaystyle\frac{5(\xi^{4}+3\xi^{3}\eta+4\xi^{2}\eta^{2}+3\xi\eta^{3}+\eta^{4})}{\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle+\frac{\xi^{3}(\xi+\eta)}{\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}s\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,h}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5(5\xi^{7}+20\xi^{6}\eta+40\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+50\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+42\xi^{3}\eta^{4}+24\xi^{2}\eta^{5}+9\xi\eta^{6}+2\eta^{7})}{2\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)

\displaystyle-\frac{5\xi^{7}+10\xi^{6}\eta+10\xi^{5}\eta^{2}+5\xi^{4}\eta^{3}+2\xi^{3}\eta^{4}}{4\eta(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6})}s\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Note that
\chi_{3}(\xi,\eta)=-s\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta)+\text{lower order terms}
.
These symbols of holomorphic type are of the following type:

\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,h}=O(\xi\eta^{-1})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,h}=O(\xi\eta^{-1})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,h}=O(\eta^{-3})\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,h}=O(1)\chi_{1}(\xi,\eta).

Similarly, to match paradifferential source terms of the mixed type in
(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{s},\mathcal{K}_{0}^{s})
, paradifferential symbols of the mixed type solve the following algebraic system:

\begin{cases}(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,a}-\zeta\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,a}-\eta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,a}=-\eta\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)-\frac{s}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,a}+\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,a}+(\zeta-\eta)\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,a}=\eta\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)+\frac{s}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\eta\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,a}-(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,a}-\zeta\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,a}=\eta\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)+\frac{s}{2}\eta\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\\
\zeta^{4}\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,a}-(\zeta-\eta)^{4}\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,a}-\eta^{4}\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,a}=\left(\frac{3}{2}\eta\zeta^{3}-\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}-\eta^{3}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\eta^{4}\right)\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)\\
+s\left(2\eta\zeta^{3}-3\eta^{2}\zeta^{2}+2\eta^{3}\zeta-\frac{1}{2}\eta^{4}\right)\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\end{cases}

where
\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)
represents the symbolic relation for the bilinear form
L
defined in (
4.11
) so that
|\eta|\ll|\zeta|
.
The solutions of the above system are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,a}=

\displaystyle\frac{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+49\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-58\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+75\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-50\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6}}{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6}}\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)+\frac{s}{2}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,a}=

\displaystyle\frac{-2\eta^{6}+11\eta^{5}\zeta-11\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}+17\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+25\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-25\eta\zeta^{5}+25\zeta^{6}}{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6}}\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)+\frac{s}{2}\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,a}=

\displaystyle\frac{10(\eta^{2}\zeta-\eta\zeta^{2}+\zeta^{3})}{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6}}\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,a}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5(2\eta^{6}-5\eta^{5}\zeta+12\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-13\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+12\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-5\eta\zeta^{5}+2\zeta^{6})}{8\eta^{6}-24\eta^{5}\zeta+74\eta^{4}\zeta^{2}-108\eta^{3}\zeta^{3}+150\eta^{2}\zeta^{4}-100\eta\zeta^{5}+50\zeta^{6}}\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta).

Since
\chi_{3}(\eta,\zeta)=-s\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta)+\text{lower order terms}
, these symbols of the mixed type are of the following type:

\mathfrak{a}^{s}_{1,a}=O(\eta^{3}\zeta^{-3})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{b}^{s}_{1,a}=O(\eta\zeta^{-1})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{c}^{s}_{1,a}=O(\eta^{-3})\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta),\quad\mathfrak{d}^{s}_{1,a}=O(1)\chi_{1}(\eta,\zeta).

Therefore, we get the upper bound of
(w^{s}_{1},r^{s}_{1})
in
{\mathcal{H}}^{0}
.

\|(w^{s}_{1},r^{s}_{1})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(\tilde{w}^{s},\tilde{r}^{s})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}.

4.4.2.
{\mathcal{H}}^{s}
normal form analysis for cubic terms of the homogeneous paradifferential system

Then we construct cubic normal form corrections
(w^{s}_{2},r^{s}_{2})
to eliminate remaining non-resonant non-perturbative cubic terms.
According to the computations for quadratic normal form corrections
(w^{s}_{1},r^{s}_{1})
, we get

\begin{cases}T_{D_{t}}w^{s}_{1}+T_{1-\bar{Y}}\partial_{\alpha}r^{s}_{1}+T_{T_{1-\bar{Y}}R_{\alpha}}w^{s}_{1}=-\mathcal{G}^{s}_{1}+\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2}+G_{s},\\
T_{D_{t}}r^{s}_{1}-i\mathcal{L}_{para}w^{s}_{1}=-\mathcal{K}^{s}_{1}+\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2}+K_{s},\end{cases}

where
(\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2},\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2})
are cubic and higher terms given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2}

\displaystyle=C_{1}T_{J^{-1}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}+C_{2}iT_{(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2}

\displaystyle=C_{3}T_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}_{\alpha\alpha}^{s}+C_{4}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\tilde{w}_{\alpha\alpha}^{s}+C_{5}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{2}_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}_{\alpha\alpha}^{s},

for some constants
C_{i}
that depend on
s
.
The non-resonant part of the cubic terms in
(\mathcal{G}^{s}_{2},\mathcal{K}^{s}_{2})
are

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{s,non}_{2}

\displaystyle=C_{2}iT_{(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\alpha}^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{s,non}_{2}

\displaystyle=C_{3}iT_{J^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1-Y)^{3}\mathbf{W}^{2}_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}_{\alpha\alpha}^{s}+C_{5}iT_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-\bar{Y})^{3}\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{2}_{\alpha}}\tilde{w}_{\alpha\alpha}^{s}.

According to the discussion in Appendix
C
, we can construct cubic modified variables
(w^{s}_{2},r^{s}_{2})=O_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2}\|(\tilde{w}^{s},\tilde{r}^{s})\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}})
to eliminate
(\mathcal{G}^{s,non}_{2},\mathcal{K}^{s,non}_{2})
.
They do not produce any extra non-perturbative terms.

For cubic terms that may have resonance,

\mathcal{G}^{s,res}_{2}=C_{1}T_{J^{-1}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s},\quad\mathcal{K}^{s,res}_{2}=C_{4}T_{J^{-\frac{5}{2}}(1-Y)|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\tilde{w}_{\alpha\alpha}^{s},

one cannot use cubic normal form directly.
Setting

w^{s}_{NF}:=\tilde{w}^{s}+w^{s}_{1}+w^{s}_{2},\quad r^{s}_{NF}:=\tilde{r}^{s}+r^{s}_{1}+r^{s}_{2}.

(w^{s}_{NF},r^{s}_{NF})
solve the system (
4.13
).
We get that

E^{0,para}_{lin}(w_{NF}^{s},r_{NF}^{s})=(1+O(\mathcal{A}_{0}))\|(w^{s}_{NF},r^{s}_{NF})\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}=(1+O(\mathcal{A}_{0}))\|(w,r)\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}.

In addition, according to the computation in Section
3.1
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{0,para}_{lin}(w_{NF}^{s},r_{NF}^{s})=-2\Re\int T_{J^{-\frac{5}{4}}}\partial_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}^{s,res}_{2}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\bar{w}^{s}_{NF}+2\Re\int r^{s}_{NF}\cdot T_{J^{\frac{1}{4}}}\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{s,res}_{2}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2C_{2}\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\tilde{r}^{s}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\bar{\tilde{w}}^{s}\,d\alpha-2C_{4}\Re\int i\tilde{r}^{s}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\bar{\tilde{w}}^{s}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(\tilde{w}^{s},\tilde{r}^{s})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(C_{2}+C_{4})\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\tilde{r}^{s}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\bar{\tilde{w}}^{s}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}^{2}.

We choose the quartic energy correction

E^{s}_{4,cor}(\tilde{w}^{s},\tilde{r}^{s})=(C_{2}+C_{4})\Re\int\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{3}{4}}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\tilde{r}}^{s}\,d\alpha.

The energy satisfies

|E^{s}_{4,cor}(\tilde{w}^{s},\tilde{r}^{s})|\lesssim\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{C^{\epsilon}_{*}}\|\tilde{r}^{s}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{0}^{2}\|r\|^{2}_{H^{s}}.

For its time derivative,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E^{s}_{4,cor}(\tilde{w}^{s},\tilde{r}^{s})=

\displaystyle 2(C_{2}+C_{4})\Re\int\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}\cdot T_{J^{-\frac{3}{4}}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\tilde{r}}^{s}_{t}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle+(C_{2}+C_{4})\Re\int\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{r}^{s}\cdot T_{\partial_{t}(J^{-\frac{3}{4}}|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2})}\partial_{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\tilde{r}}^{s}\,d\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(C_{2}+C_{4})\Re\int iT_{J^{-\frac{9}{4}}(1-\bar{Y})|\mathbf{W}_{\alpha}|^{2}}\tilde{r}^{s}\cdot\partial_{\alpha}^{2}\bar{\tilde{w}}^{s}\,d\alpha+O\left({\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\right)\|(w,r)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}^{2}.

Hence, choosing the modified energy defined in (
4.14
), we finish the proof of Proposition
4.2
.

As a direct corollary of Proposition
4.1
and Proposition
4.2
, we obtain the modified energy estimate of (
4.6
).

Proposition 4.3
.

Assume that
\mathcal{A}_{0}\lesssim 1
and
\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}\in L^{2}_{t}([0,T])
for some time
T>0
, then if
(\hat{w},\hat{r})
solve the homogeneous paradifferential system (
4.6
) on
[0,T]
, there exists an energy functional
\tilde{E}^{s,para}_{lin}(w,r)
such that on
[0,T]
for
s\geq 0
, we have the following two properties:

(1)

Norm equivalence:

\tilde{E}^{s,para}_{lin}(\hat{w},\hat{r})=(1+O({\mathcal{A}_{0}}))\|(\hat{w},\hat{r})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}^{2}.

(2)

The time derivative of
\tilde{E}^{s,para}_{lin}(\hat{w},\hat{r})
is bounded by

\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{E}^{s,para}_{lin}(\hat{w},\hat{r})\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}\|(\hat{w},\hat{r})\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}^{2}.

Given
(\mathbf{W},R)
that solve the hydroelastic waves (
1.7
) on
[0,T]
, since the corresponding modified variables
(\mathbf{W}_{NF},R_{NF})
satisfy the bound (
4.5
) and solve (
4.6
).
We get that

E_{s}(\mathbf{W},R):=\tilde{E}^{s,para}_{lin}(\mathbf{W}_{NF},R_{NF})

is the desired modified energy that finishes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

5.
Low regularity well-posedness of the hydroelastic waves

In this final section, we give an outline of how to prove the local well-posedness of differentiated hydroelastic waves (
1.7
).
For a detailed exposition of the argument, we refer the interested reader to Section
7
of the low-regularity well-posedness theory for gravity water waves
[
3
]
.

First, for any initial data with high regularity
\mathcal{H}^{\frac{5}{2}k}
for
k>2
, a solution exists based on the result in
[
52
]
.
Moreover, by the modified energy estimate in Theorem
1.1
, if
\mathcal{A}_{0}(t)\lesssim 1
and
{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}(t)\in L^{2}([0,T])
, using Gronwall’s inequality

\|(\mathbf{W},R)(t)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{5}{2}k}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\exp{\ \left\{\int_{0}^{t}{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}(\tau)\,d\tau\right\}}\|(\mathbf{W},R)(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{5}{2}k}}.

By Sobolev embedding, if
(\mathbf{W},R)(t)\in{\mathcal{H}}^{s}
for
s>\frac{3}{4}
, then
\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}(t)\lesssim\|(\mathbf{W},R)(t)\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{s}}
.

We then construct rough solutions as the unique limit of smooth solutions.
Given solution
(\mathbf{W}_{0},R_{0})\in{\mathcal{H}}^{s}
for
s>\frac{3}{4}
, we perform frequency truncation on the initial data, and obtain
(\mathbf{W}_{0}^{k},R_{0}^{k})=P_{<k}(\mathbf{W}_{0},R_{0})\in\mathcal{H}^{\frac{5}{2}k}
for
k>2
.
The corresponding solutions
(\mathbf{W}^{k},R^{k})
exist with a uniform lifespan bound.
Utilizing the method of frequency envelopes (see Definition
7.1
in
[
3
]
) and energy estimate Theorem
1.1
, we obtain an upper bound for
(\mathbf{W}^{k},R^{k})
in
{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{5}{2}k}
for
k>2
.
On the other hand,

(w^{k},r^{k})=(\partial_{k}W^{k},\partial_{k}Q^{k}-R^{k}\partial_{k}W^{k})

solve the corresponding linearized equations around
(\mathbf{W}^{k},R^{k})
.
Using the modified energy estimate for the linearized hydroelastic waves Theorem
3.1
,
one can establish the bound for the difference
(\mathbf{W}^{k+1}-\mathbf{W}^{k},R^{k+1}-R^{k})
in
\mathcal{H}^{0}
.
Summing over
k
and using interpolation, the sequence
(\mathbf{W}^{k},R^{k})
converges to a solution
(\mathbf{W},R)
with uniform
\mathcal{H}^{s}
bound in time interval
[0,T]
.
This process also establishes uniqueness, as the solution is defined as the unique limit of regular solutions.

Finally, we demonstrate the continuous dependence on initial data for rough solutions.
The proof follows essentially the same argument as the final paragraph of Section
7.2
in
[
3
]
using frequency envelopes, and is therefore omitted here.

Appendix A
Paradifferential estimates

In this first part of the appendix, we list the definition of norms and recall paraproducts and paradifferential estimates we have used in previous sections.
Many of these definitions and estimates are relatively standard.
They can be found in for instance
[
9
,
8
]
or the textbooks
[
16
,
36
]
.

A.1.
Norms and function spaces

We recall the Littlewood-Paley frequency decomposition,

I=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}P_{k},

where for each
k\geq 1
,
P_{k}
are smooth symbols localized at frequency
2^{k}
, and
P_{0}
selects the low frequency components
|\xi|\leq 1
.

(1)

Let
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and
p,q\in[1,\infty]
.
The non-homogeneous Besov space
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R})
is defined as the space of all tempered distributions
u
such that

\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}}:=\left\|(2^{ks}\|P_{k}u\|_{L^{p}})_{k=0}^{\infty}\right\|_{l^{q}}<+\infty.

(2)

When
p=q=\infty
, Besov space
B^{s}_{\infty,\infty}
coincides with the
Zygmund space

C^{s}_{*}
.
When
p=q=2
, the Besov space
B^{s}_{2,2}
becomes the
Sobolev space

H^{s}
.

(3)

Let
1\leq p_{1}\leq p_{2}\leq\infty
,
1\leq r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq\infty
, then for any real number
s
,

B^{s}_{p_{1},r_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow B^{s-(\frac{1}{p_{1}}-\frac{1}{p_{2}})}_{p_{2},r_{2}}(\mathbb{R}).

As a special case when
p_{1}=r_{1}=2
and
p_{2}=r_{2}=\infty
,

(A.1)

H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C^{s}_{*}(\mathbb{R})\quad\forall s,

the Sobolev space
H^{s+\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R})
can be embedding into the Zygmund space
C^{s}_{*}(\mathbb{R})
.

(4)

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the space of all functions such that
\partial_{x}^{j}u\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
0\leq j\leq k
.
For
\rho=k+\sigma
with
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\sigma\in(0,1)
, we denote
W^{\rho,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the
space of all function
u\in W^{k,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that the
\partial_{x}^{k}u
is
\sigma
- Hölder continuous on
\mathbb{R}
.

(5)

The Zygmund space
C^{s}_{*}(\mathbb{R})
is just the Hölder space
W^{s,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
when
s\in(0,\infty)\backslash\mathbb{N}
.
One has the embedding properties

\displaystyle C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}),\quad s>0;\qquad L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C_{*}^{s},\quad s<0;

\displaystyle C_{*}^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow C_{*}^{s_{2}}(\mathbb{R}),\quad H^{s_{1}}(\mathbb{R})\hookrightarrow H^{s_{2}}(\mathbb{R}),\qquad s_{1}>s_{2}.

A.2.
Paradifferential and Moser type estimates

Definition A.1
.

(1)

Let
\rho\in[0,\infty)
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
.

\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the space of locally bounded functions
a(x,\xi)
on
\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\})
, which are
C^{\infty}
with respect to
\xi
for
\xi\neq 0
and such that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\xi\neq 0
, the function
x\mapsto\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(x,\xi)
belongs to
W^{\rho,\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and there exists a constant
C_{k}
with

\forall|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2},\quad\|\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(\cdot,\xi)\|_{W^{\rho,\infty}}\leq C_{k}(1+|\xi|)^{m-k}.

Let
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, we define the semi-norm

M^{m}_{\rho}(a)=\sup_{k\leq\frac{3}{2}+\rho}\sup_{|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2}}\|(1+|\xi|)^{k-m}\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(\cdot,\xi)\|_{W^{\rho,\infty}}.

(2)

Given
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R})
, let
C^{\infty}
functions
\chi(\theta,\eta)
and
\psi(\eta)
be such that for some
0<\epsilon_{1}<\epsilon_{2}<1
,

\displaystyle\chi(\theta,\eta)=1,\text{ if }|\theta|\leq\epsilon_{1}(1+|\eta|),\qquad\chi(\theta,\eta)=0,\text{ if }|\theta|\geq\epsilon_{2}(1+|\eta|),

\displaystyle\psi(\eta)=0,\text{ if }|\eta|\leq\frac{1}{5},\qquad\psi(\eta)=1,\text{ if }|\eta|\geq\frac{1}{4}.

We define the paradifferential operator
T_{a}
by

\displaystyle\widehat{T_{a}u}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\chi(\xi-\eta,\eta)\hat{a}(\xi-\eta,\eta)\psi(\eta)\hat{u}(\eta)d\eta,

where
\hat{a}(\theta,\xi)
is the Fourier transform of a with respect to the variable
x
.

(3)

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
, an operator is said to be of order
m
if, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, it is bounded from
H^{s}
to
H^{s-m}
.

(4)

Let
\rho\in(-\infty,0)
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
.

\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the space of distributions
a(x,\xi)
on
\mathbb{R}\times(\mathbb{R}\backslash\{0\})
, which are
C^{\infty}
with respect to
\xi
for
\xi\neq 0
and such that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\xi\neq 0
, the function
x\mapsto\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(x,\xi)
belongs to
C^{\rho}_{*}(\mathbb{R})
and there exists a constant
C_{k}
with

\forall|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2},\quad\|\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(\cdot,\xi)\|_{C^{\rho}_{*}}\leq C_{k}(1+|\xi|)^{m-k}.

Let
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, we define the semi-norm

M^{m}_{\rho}(a)=\sup_{k\leq\frac{3}{2}+|\rho|}\sup_{|\xi|\geq\frac{1}{2}}\|(1+|\xi|)^{k-m}\partial_{\xi}^{k}a(\cdot,\xi)\|_{C^{\rho}_{*}}.

We recall the basic symbolic calculus for paradifferential operators in the following result.

Lemma A.2

(Symbolic calculus,
[
36
]
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R}
and
\rho\in[0,+\infty)
.

(1)

If
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{0}
, then the paradifferential operator
T_{a}
is of order m. Moreover, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a positive constant
K
such that

(A.2)

\|T_{a}\|_{H^{s}\rightarrow H^{s-m}}\leq KM^{m}_{0}(a).

(2)

If
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, and
b\in\Gamma^{m^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\rho}
with
\rho>0
, then the operator
T_{a}T_{b}-T_{a\sharp b}
is of order
m+m^{{}^{\prime}}-\rho
, where the composition

a\sharp b:=\sum_{\alpha<\rho}\frac{(-i)^{\alpha}}{\alpha!}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}a(x,\xi)\partial^{\alpha}_{x}b(x,\xi).

Moreover, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a positive constant
K
such that

(A.3)

\|T_{a}T_{b}-T_{a\sharp b}\|_{H^{s}\rightarrow H^{s-m-m^{{}^{\prime}}+\rho}}\leq K\left(M^{m}_{\rho}(a)M^{m^{{}^{\prime}}}_{0}(b)+M^{m}_{0}(a)M^{m^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\rho}(b)\right).

(3)

Let
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
with
\rho>0
.
Denote by
(T_{a})^{*}
the adjoint operator of
T_{a}
and by
\bar{a}
the complex conjugate of
a
.
Then
(T_{a})^{*}-T_{a^{*}}
is of order
m-\rho
, where

a^{*}=\sum_{\alpha<\rho}\frac{1}{i^{\alpha}\alpha!}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\bar{a}.

Moreover, for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists a positive constant
K
such that

(A.4)

\|(T_{a})^{*}-T_{a^{*}}\|_{H^{s}\rightarrow H^{s-m+\rho}}\leq KM^{m}_{\rho}(a).

In particular, if
a
is a function that is independent of
\xi
, then
(T_{a})^{*}=T_{\bar{a}}
.

In Besov spaces, we have similar results for symbolic calculus.

Lemma A.3

(
[
51
]
)
.

Let
m,m^{{}^{\prime}},s\in\mathbb{R}
,
q\in[1,\infty]
and
\rho\in[0,+\infty)
.

(1)

If
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{0}
, then there exists a positive constant
K
such that

\|T_{a}\|_{B^{s}_{\infty,q}\rightarrow B^{s-m}_{\infty,q}}\leq KM^{m}_{0}(a).

(2)

If
a\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, and
b\in\Gamma^{m}_{\rho}
, then there exists a positive constant
K
such that

(A.5)

\|T_{a}T_{b}-T_{a\sharp b}\|_{B^{s}_{\infty,q}\rightarrow B^{s-m-m^{{}^{\prime}}+\rho}_{\infty,q}}\leq K\left(M^{m}_{\rho}(a)M^{m^{{}^{\prime}}}_{0}(b)+M^{m}_{0}(a)M^{m^{{}^{\prime}}}_{\rho}(b)\right).

In particular, when
q=\infty
, above symbolic calculus results hold for Zygmund spaces
C^{s}_{*}
.

When
a
is only a function of
x
,
T_{a}u
is the low high paraproduct.
We then define

\Pi(a,u):=au-T_{a}u-T_{u}a

to be the high-high paraproduct.
For later use, we record below some estimates for paraproducts.

Lemma A.4
.

(1)

Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
.
If
\alpha+\beta>0
, then

(A.6)

\displaystyle\|\Pi(a,u)\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{H^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.7)

\displaystyle\|\Pi(a,u)\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{W^{\alpha,4}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{W^{\beta,4}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.8)

\displaystyle\|\Pi(a,u)\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\beta}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{\beta}(\mathbb{R})}.

(2)

Let
m>0
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

(A.9)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{H^{s-m}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{-m}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.10)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.11)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{H^{s-m}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{W^{-m,4}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{W^{s,4}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.12)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{H^{s-m}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{H^{-m}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.13)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{C_{*}^{s-m}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{C_{*}^{-m}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(A.14)

\displaystyle\|T_{a}u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}(\mathbb{R})}.

(3)

Let a smooth function
F\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{C}^{N})
satisfying
F(0)=0
.
There exists a nondecreasing function
\mathcal{F}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that,

(A.15)

\displaystyle\|F(u)\|_{H^{s}}\leq\mathcal{F}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}})\|u\|_{H^{s}},\quad s\geq 0,

(A.16)

\displaystyle\|F(u)\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\leq\mathcal{F}(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}})\|u\|_{C_{*}^{s}},\quad s>0.

(4)

Let
s_{1}>s_{2}>0
, then

(A.17)

\|uv\|_{C^{-s_{2}}_{*}}\lesssim\|u\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{-s_{2}}_{*}}.

When we need to commute the para-coefficients and the balanced paraproducts, we need the following results of the para-associativity.

Lemma A.5

(Para-associativity,
[
3
]
)
.

For
s+s_{2}>0
,
s+s_{1}+s_{2}>0
, and
s_{2}<1
, we have

\displaystyle\|T_{f}\Pi(v,u)-\Pi(v,T_{f}u)\|_{C_{*}^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}\|u\|_{C^{s}_{*}},

\displaystyle\|T_{f}\Pi(v,u)-\Pi(v,T_{f}u)\|_{H^{s+s_{1}+s_{2}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}\|u\|_{H^{s}},

\displaystyle\|T_{f}\Pi(v,u)-\Pi(v,T_{f}u)\|_{W^{s+s_{1}+s_{2},4}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C^{s_{1}}_{*}}\|v\|_{C^{s_{2}}_{*}}\|u\|_{W^{s,4}}.

This result shows that para-coefficients act like constant coefficients modulo perturbative error, so that we can freely commute them with balanced paraproducts.

Finally, to paralinearize functions in Besov spaces, we need the following results in Section 2.8 of
[
16
]
.

Lemma A.6

(Paralinearization
[
16
]
)
.

Let
s,\rho>0
, and
F(u)
be a smooth function of
u
.
Assume that
\rho
is not an integer.
Let
p,r_{1},r_{2}\in[1,\infty]
and such that
r_{2}\geq r_{1}
.
Let
r\in[1,\infty]
be defined by
\frac{1}{r}=\min\{1,\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}\}
.
Then for any
u\in B^{s}_{p,r_{1}}\cap B^{\rho}_{\infty,r_{2}}
,

\|F(u)-F(0)-T_{F^{{}^{\prime}}(u)}u\|_{B^{s+\rho}_{p,r}}\leq C(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}})\|u\|_{B^{\rho}_{\infty,r_{2}}}\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p.r_{1}}}.

We remark that this lemma also works for multivariable functions
F
.
We simply need to replace
F^{{}^{\prime}}
by partial derivatives of
F
.
See for instance Lemma
3.26
in
[
7
]
.
We will apply this paralinearization result with
p=r=r_{1}=r_{2}=\infty
, so that this is an estimate in Zygmund spaces.

A.3.
Paradiffential estimates for bilinear forms

In the following, we consider the estimates for the bilinear forms.
Consider a pseudodifferential operator
A(x,D)
with symbol
a(x,\xi)
and a function
u(x)
.
Let
\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),\chi_{2}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
be two non-negative smooth functions

(A.18)

\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\left\{\begin{aligned} 1,\text{ when }|\theta_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{20}|\theta_{2}|,\\
0,\text{ when }|\theta_{1}|\geq\frac{1}{10}|\theta_{2}|,\end{aligned}\right.

(A.19)

\chi_{2}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\text{ when }\frac{1}{10}\leq\frac{|\theta_{1}|}{|\theta_{2}|}\leq 10,\\
&0,\text{ when }|\theta_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{20}|\theta_{2}|\text{ or }|\theta_{2}|\leq\frac{1}{20}|\theta_{1}|,\end{aligned}\right.

and such that
\chi_{1}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})+\chi_{1}(\theta_{2},\theta_{1})+\chi_{2}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=1
.
For bilinear forms
B(u,v)
with symbol
m(\xi,\eta)
, we can define the paradifferential bilinear forms in Weyl quantization:

•

Low-high part and high-high part of the holomorphic bilinear forms:

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{lh}(u,v)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\chi_{1}\left(\xi,\eta+\xi\right)m(\xi,\eta)\hat{u}(\xi)\hat{v}(\eta)d\xi,

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{hh}(u,v)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\zeta=\xi+\eta}\chi_{2}\left(\xi,\eta+\xi\right)m(\xi,\eta)\hat{u}(\xi)\hat{v}(\eta)d\xi.

•

Low-high part and high-high part of the mixed bilinear forms:

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{lh}(u,v)}(\eta)

\displaystyle=1_{\eta>0}\int_{\eta=\zeta-\xi}\chi_{1}\left(\xi,\zeta-\xi\right)m(\xi,\zeta)\bar{\hat{u}}(\xi)\hat{v}(\zeta)d\xi,

\displaystyle\widehat{B_{hh}(u,v)}(\eta)

\displaystyle=1_{\eta>0}\int_{\eta=\zeta-\xi}\chi_{2}\left(\xi,\zeta-\xi\right)m(\xi,\zeta)\bar{\hat{u}}(\xi)\hat{v}(\zeta)d\xi.

These represent low-high and high-high paradifferential parts of the bilinear forms
B(u,v)
, respectively
{\mathbf{P}}B(\bar{u},v)
, restricted to the holomorphic class.
We will always assume that bilinear symbols
m
are homogeneous, and smooth away from
(0,0)
.

When the bilinear symbol
m
is homogeneous, we have the following direct generalization of Lemma
A.4
for bilinear forms, see Coifman-Meyer
[
19
]
, Kenig-Stein
[
31
]
, Muscalu
[
38
]
, and Muscalu-Tao-Thiele
[
37
]
.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
B^{\mu}(f,g)
be a homogeneous bilinear form of order
\mu\geq 0
as above.
For the high-high bilinear forms, when
\alpha+\beta+\mu>0
,
\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}=\mu

(A.20)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{hh}(f,g)\|_{H^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\mu_{1}}}\|g\|_{H^{\beta+\mu_{2}}},

(A.21)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{hh}(f,g)\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{\alpha+\mu_{1}}}\|g\|_{C_{*}^{\beta+\mu_{2}}}.

For the estimate of low-high bilinear form,

(A.22)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{H^{s-m}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{-m}}\|g\|_{H^{s+\mu}},

(A.23)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|g\|_{H^{s+\mu}},

(A.24)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{C_{*}^{s-m}}\lesssim\|f\|_{C_{*}^{-m}}\|g\|_{C_{*}^{s+\mu}},

(A.25)

\displaystyle\|B^{\mu}_{lh}(f,g)\|_{C_{*}^{s}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{C_{*}^{s+\mu}}.

Appendix B
Hydroelastic waves related estimates

In the second part of the appendix we recall Sobolev and Zygmund estimates for auxiliary functions in hydroelastic waves.
The proofs can be found in Section 2 of
[
48
]
.

We first recall the estimate for the frequency shift
a=i\left(\bar{\mathbf{P}}[\bar{R}R_{\alpha}]-{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{R}_{\alpha}]\right)
and the advection velocity
b=2\Re{\mathbf{P}}[(1-\bar{Y})R]
.

Lemma B.1
.

The frequency shift
a
satisfies the estimate

(B.1)

\|a\|_{H^{\epsilon}}\lesssim\|R\|^{2}_{W^{\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon,4}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}.

The advection velocity
b
satisfies estimates

(B.2)

\|b\|_{C^{\frac{1}{4}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{7}{4}},\quad\quad\|b\|_{W^{\frac{1}{2},4}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}},

as well as the Sobolev estimate

(B.3)

\|b\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim_{{\mathcal{A}_{0}}}\|R\|_{H^{s}},\quad s>0.

Next, we recall estimates for
Y:=\frac{\mathbf{W}}{1+\mathbf{W}}
and
M=2\Re{\mathbf{P}}[R\bar{Y}_{\alpha}-\bar{R}_{\alpha}Y]
.

Lemma B.2
.

For
s>0
, the auxiliary function
Y
satisfies

(B.4)

\|Y\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s}},\quad\|Y\|_{C^{s}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{C^{s}_{*}},\quad\|Y\|_{W^{s,4}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s}}.

In particular,
\|Y\|_{C^{\frac{7}{4}}_{*}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{7}{4}}
and
\|Y\|_{W^{2,4}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}
.
Moreover, one can write

(B.5)

Y=T_{(1-Y)^{2}}\mathbf{W}+E,

where the error
E
satisfies the bounds

\|E\|_{C_{*}^{s+\frac{7}{4}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{7}{4}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{C_{*}^{s}},\quad\|E\|_{H^{s+\frac{7}{4}}}\lesssim_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{7}{4}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{H^{s}}.

The auxiliary function
M
satisfies bounds

(B.6)

\|M\|_{C^{\frac{1}{4}}_{*}}\lesssim\mathcal{A}_{1}\mathcal{A}_{\frac{7}{4}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}},\quad\|M\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim{\mathcal{A}_{\sharp,\frac{7}{4}}^{2}}.

Appendix C
Discussion on the resonances

In the final part of the appendix, we have a brief discussion on the three-wave and four-wave interactions in the analysis.
We conclude with a brief discussion of why resonances do not impede the analysis, so that the normal forms can remove those non-perturbative quadratic terms and certain non-resonant parts of cubic terms.

Recall that the linearization of the hydroelastic waves around the zero solution is given by (
1.11
), and its dispersion relation is then

(C.1)

\tau=\pm|\xi|^{\frac{5}{2}},\quad\xi<0.

Thus resonances in bilinear interactions correspond to zeroes of the expression
|\xi|^{\frac{5}{2}}\pm|\eta|^{\frac{5}{2}}\pm|\xi+\eta|^{\frac{5}{2}}
.
A direct calculation shows:

\displaystyle\prod_{\pm}|\xi|^{\frac{5}{2}}\pm|\eta|^{\frac{5}{2}}\pm|\xi+\eta|^{\frac{5}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\xi^{2}\eta^{2}[25(\xi^{3}+2\xi^{2}\eta+2\xi\eta^{2}+\eta^{3})^{2}-4\xi^{3}\eta^{3}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\xi^{2}\eta^{2}(25\xi^{6}+100\xi^{5}\eta+200\xi^{4}\eta^{2}+246\xi^{3}\eta^{3}+200\xi^{2}\eta^{4}+100\xi\eta^{5}+25\eta^{6}),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\xi^{2}\eta^{2}[25(\xi^{4}+\eta^{4})(\xi+\eta)^{2}+50\xi^{2}\eta^{2}(\xi+\eta)^{2}+\xi^{2}\eta^{2}(125\xi^{2}+146\xi\eta+125\eta^{2})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\xi^{2}\eta^{2}\left\{25(\xi^{2}+\eta^{2})^{2}(\xi+\eta)^{2}+\xi^{2}\eta^{2}\left[\left(25\xi+\frac{73}{25}\eta\right)^{2}+\left(125-\frac{73^{2}}{125}\right)\eta^{2}\right]\right\}\geq 0.

Equality holds only if
\xi=\eta=0
.
Since the bilinear symbols for our paradifferential quadratic normal forms involve denominators that are non-zero for
\xi\neq\eta
(as is the case for low-high or balanced terms), three-wave resonances cannot occur.
Thus, the bilinear symbols are well-defined.

We then consider the situation of four-wave resonances.
For trilinear terms, suppose the frequencies of three factors are
\xi_{1}
,
\xi_{2}
and
\xi_{3}
, then the output frequency is
\xi_{0}=-\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}-\xi_{3}
.
Four-wave resonance occurs if

|\xi_{0}|^{\frac{5}{2}}\pm|\xi_{1}|^{\frac{5}{2}}\pm|\xi_{2}|^{\frac{5}{2}}\pm|\xi_{3}|^{\frac{5}{2}}=0

for certain choices of plus or minus sign.
Since
f(\xi)=|\xi|^{\frac{5}{2}}
is strictly convex, this equality is possible only if the frequencies are paired, i.e.

\xi_{i}=\pm\xi_{j},\quad\xi_{k}=\pm\xi_{l},\quad\{i,j,k,l\}=\{0,1,2,3\},

and
\pm
signs are also chosen property for the cancellation.
For non-perturbative cubic terms that we want to apply cubic normal forms for elimination, the frequencies of each factor satisfy either

|\xi_{i}|\ll|\xi_{j}|<|\xi_{k}|,\quad\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\},

or

|\xi_{i}|,|\xi_{j}|\ll|\xi_{k}|,\quad\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\},\quad\xi_{i},\xi_{j}\text{ have the same sign}.

In either of the situations, four-wave resonances cannot happen.
Consequently, four-wave resonances are avoided in the construction of the cubic normal forms and quartic energy corrections.

To compute the expression of cubic normal forms or quartic energy corrections, one will have to solve
8\times 8
algebraic systems, as in the computation of quadratic normal forms or cubic energy corrections.
While computing the exact expressions of cubic normal forms or quartic energy corrections involves solving complex algebraic systems, their exact forms are not critical.
Their primary function is to eliminate non-perturbative terms.
Hence, we will not compute them explicitly in the analysis.

In the final paragraph, we give a qualitative explanation of why these cubic normal forms or quartic modified energies can remove non-perturbative cubic terms or quartic energies in our analysis.
Each normal form transformation generates higher-order terms with lower derivative counts.
For example, when we compute quadratic normal forms to eliminate all non-perturbative quadratic terms, it produces extra cubic and higher terms with at least one lower order.
Similarly, when we compute cubic normal forms to eliminate remaining non-perturbative cubic terms, it produces extra quartic and higher terms with at least one lower order.
Given that hydroelastic waves are dispersive equations of order
\frac{5}{2}
, and non-perturbative source terms have order of at most
\frac{3}{2}
, the remaining quartic and higher terms of order less than or equal to zero are perturbative after performing quadratic and cubic normal forms.
The same logic applies to the integral corrections.
Every time we compute the integral corrections, it produces extra higher integral terms with lower order.
Hence, after the construction of cubic and quartic integral corrections, the remaining quintic and higher integral terms are perturbative.
